# buildbot-sheathing research log

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 57 result: on a held-out seed the &beta; = 0.5 rule replicates (H226 held) and coverage is flat from &beta; = 0 to 0.5 (H228 held), but &beta; = 1 puts one H1 press over spec (0.0315 mm; H227 refuted), and every flip the budget allows is a leg pitch joint that moves the nose 0.27 to 0.29 mm across the wall, the direction no rule budgets

`experiments/m57_beta/` (run, results, figure), `reproduce.py` (m57 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 768 s on 4 processes. One 2-load smoke run (`M57_N=2`) ran after the hypotheses were committed and before the full run; it is not reported.

- **Check.** On the first 4 loads of each robot (780 cases), milestone 56's own `weighted_rule` at &beta; = 0.5 and the one-pass evaluation agree on every plan/no-plan and how, and the residual differs by 0.0 mm.
- **Replication (H226 held).** Section D (48 Latin-hypercube loads, seed 57; 6 random-direction 0.2 N errors, seed 57), &beta; = 0.5: worst |y| G1 0.0105 mm, H1 0.0178 mm; coverage G1 4017 of 4020 (99.93%), H1 5375 of 5400 (99.54%); smallest Y &minus; flip shift &minus;6.9e-18 mm (rounding).
- **The frontier.** Coverage (G1 / H1) and worst |y|: &beta; 0: 99.93% / 99.54%, 0.0105 / 0.0100 mm; 0.25: same coverage, 0.0105 / 0.0175 mm; 0.5: same, 0.0105 / 0.0178 mm; 0.75: 99.93% / **100%**, 0.0278 / 0.0178 mm; 1.0: 99.93% / 100%, 0.0278 / **0.0315 mm**. The G1's 3 unplanned cases have no plan on any side at any &beta;; the H1's 25 all bind on the left knee and come back only at &beta; = 0.75. Coverage at 0.25 equals coverage at 0.5 on both robots (H228 held), and so does &beta; = 0.
- **&beta; = 1 (H227 refuted).** One H1 press, pose (1.2 m, &minus;0.15) at 12.21 N, 182.6 degrees, error 1, leaves 0.0315 mm along the wall. The bound held there (the flip shift stayed under Y); the spec was lost because Y used all 0.03 mm and the windup and calibration terms add on top, as the half-and-half split assumed.
- **Across the wall (reported, not registered).** The rule budgets only y. Worst |z| of a planned press: &beta; 0: G1 0.026, H1 0.019 mm; &beta; 0.25: 0.267, 0.281 mm; 0.5: 0.277, 0.289 mm; 1.0: 0.278, 0.525 mm. Every planned press with |z| over 0.03 mm (&beta; 0.5: 4 G1 and 8 H1 of 9392) has a held or side flip between plan and robot, on a knee or ankle pitch joint (H1's right ankle, G1's knees and ankle pitch): at these poses those joints move the nose little along the wall and a lot in z. The &beta; = 1 H1 worst, 0.525 mm, is two flips with the left knee in band.
- **What it means.** On this seed the budget buys nothing until 0.75: &beta; = 0 (plan only when no joint whose flip moves the nose along the wall sits in its band) plans exactly as many presses as 0.5, with the same or smaller worst |y| and a tenth of the worst |z|. The flips the budget admits are cheap in y and expensive in z, so a y-only bound reads them as free. The fix is a bound on the full in-plane shift: each joint's cost through both the y and z rows (or their norm), with a z budget, which is milestone 56's construction with one more Jacobian row.
- **Not measured.** What spec the z direction needs (whether 0.3 mm in z matters depends on what the press is aligning to); a second held-out seed (one seed, 48 loads, 9420 in-envelope cases, so the 25-case H1 gap at &beta; &le; 0.5 is 0.46% with wide error); statics only; constant sensor bias; &mu; 0.5 not measured.
- **Next.** Put both rows into the bound (Y<sub>y</sub> and Y<sub>z</sub>, each with its own budget) and rerun sections A to D at &beta; = 0 and 0.5; or preload the H1 left knee, the only joint that still binds at &beta; &le; 0.5.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 57 planned: on a held-out seed, where does the budget share &beta; of milestone 56's weighted rule put coverage against the worst residual, and does &beta; = 1 (the whole spec for flips) still keep every press in spec?

- **Why.** Milestone 56 set &beta; = 0.5 before its run (half the 0.03 mm spec for backlash flips, half for windup and the calibration) and did not tune it; its "Not measured" and "Next" asked for a sweep on a held-out seed to place the coverage-accuracy frontier.
- **Known before the run (from reading the code, no number computed).** In `m56_weighted/run.py` the squeeze is chosen to minimise Y whatever the budget, and among side combinations that pass the smallest Y wins, which is the combination with the smallest Y overall when that one passes. So one pass per case (Y and residual on the pose's sides, and the smallest-Y side combination with its residual) gives the rule's plan at every &beta;: pose sides if Y<sub>0</sub> &le; &beta; &middot; 0.03 mm, else the best combination if it passes (searched only when the pose-side plan has a leg cost or no plan, as milestone 56 does), else none. Coverage is then non-decreasing in &beta; by construction; the worst residual need not be monotone.
- **Model.** Milestone 56's setup and rule unchanged (its run imported), with &beta; in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0}. Section D, held out: 48 new Latin-hypercube loads (seed 57) with 6 random-direction 0.2 N sensor errors (seed 57), not seen by milestones 54 to 56. Check: at &beta; = 0.5 on the first 4 loads of each robot, milestone 56's own `weighted_rule` is called and must give the same plan/no-plan and residual. Also reported, not registered: the residual across the wall (z) of the planned cases, which no rule budgets.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H226 (held-out replication, &beta; = 0.5).** Every planned in-envelope pose-case is inside 0.03 mm along the wall, coverage inside milestone 51's envelope is at least 99% on each robot, and the flip shift never beats Y (by more than 1e-9 mm). Refuted if any of the three fails.
  - **H227 (&beta; = 1).** With the whole spec given to flips, every planned in-envelope pose-case is still inside 0.03 mm on both robots. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H228 (a flat frontier below 0.5).** Coverage at &beta; = 0.25 is within 0.5 percentage points of coverage at &beta; = 0.5 on each robot. Refuted if either robot loses more.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 56 result: weighting the held-or-free margin by each flip's cost at the nose keeps every planned press in spec (worst 0.0178 mm; H223 held), wins back every press milestone 55 dropped on the G1 and all but 24, 6 and 12 on the H1 (coverage 99.6 to 100%; H224 held), and the flip bound is never beaten (H225 held)

`experiments/m56_weighted/` (run, results, figure), `src/buildbot_sheathing/preload.py` (`reachable_states`, `flip_cost`), `tests/test_flipcost.py` (6 tests: zero cost far from the threshold, half and full gap, and the bound against corner and 5000 uniform torque draws), `reproduce.py` (m56 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. Run time 1710 s on 4 processes. One 2-load smoke run (`M56_N=2`) ran after the hypotheses were committed and before the full run; it is not reported.

- **The rule.** Each joint's worst nose shift from a backlash flip, c<sub>j</sub> = |w<sub>j</sub>| &times; the largest change of its `held_bias` term over the held states its torque band [&tau;&#770; &minus; r<sub>j</sub>, &tau;&#770; + r<sub>j</sub>] can reach at the two press ends, with w the wall row of the nose Jacobian; the plan passes when Y = &Sigma; c<sub>j</sub> &le; 0.015 mm (half the 0.03 mm spec). Milestone 55's rule is the zero-budget case.
- **Spec (H223 held).** Every planned in-envelope pose-case in all three sections is inside 0.03 mm: worst G1 0.0107 / 0.0110 / 0.0125 mm and H1 0.0178 / 0.0147 / 0.0089 mm in sections A / B / C (milestone 55: G1 0.0107 / 0.0110, H1 0.0099 / 0.0091). The worst H1 case grows by 0.008 mm: that is the budget being spent.
- **Coverage (H224 held).** G1 4002 of 4002, 3924 of 3924 and 3990 of 3990 (100%); H1 5412 of 5436 (99.6%), 5418 of 5424 (99.9%), 5412 of 5424 (99.8%). Paired case by case with milestone 55 on sections A and B, the weighted rule plans every case milestone 55 planned and wins back 637 of the 667 it dropped (G1 308 of 308, H1 329 of 359), worst residual of a won-back case 0.0147 mm.
- **The bound (H225 held).** Over 28 158 planned cases, 156 have a held or side flip between plan and robot (the rule now allows them where they are cheap), and on every planned case the flip shift (|y| minus |y| with the plan's held states) is at most Y (smallest Y &minus; shift &minus;2.6e-18 mm, rounding). The bound is tight: on single-joint flips the shift equals Y to rounding (largest shift/Y 1.000), and some flips reduce |y| (shift down to &minus;0.008 mm), since the triangle inequality ignores sign.
- **What still binds.** The 42 H1 cases left: 32 bind on the left knee (one or two joints in band, Y 0.0166 to 0.0246 mm, over the budget) and no side combination of 16 passes; 10 have the right elbow in band at 0.167 mm, which the hold sides cannot move (not side-searched). The G1 waist and H1 torso, milestone 55's binding joints, never bind here: of the 302 planned cases with Y &gt; 0 the largest cost is on a knee or ankle every time.
- **What it means.** On these robots most joints sit so that half a backlash gap barely moves the nose along the wall; the waist and torso cost nothing at the budget. A robot can compute the weighted rule from the measured load alone (three extra settle solves for G and one Jacobian row), and it gives the spec guarantee for the backlash term with 0.4% or less of presses lost. The budget split (half the spec for flips, half for windup and calibration) was set before the run, not tuned.
- **Not measured.** Whether &beta; = 0.5 is the best split (a sweep would trade the H1's last 0.4% against the worst residual); the time the side search takes on a robot; a sensor error that is not a constant bias; the residual across the wall (z) was not given a budget, only y; statics only; &mu; 0.5 not measured for these soles.
- **Next.** Sweep &beta; on a held-out seed to place the coverage-accuracy frontier, and add the z residual to the bound; or preload the H1 knee and elbow (the only joints still binding) through the stance or the squeeze direction.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 56 planned: weight the held-or-free margin by what a flip costs at the nose: does a budget on the bound of the flips' nose shift keep every press in spec and win back milestone 55's 3 to 4% of dropped presses?

- **Why.** Milestone 55's robust rule asks every joint's planned torque to sit further than r<sub>j</sub> = B||G<sub>j</sub>|| from the 0.2 N m friction torque. That fixed milestone 54's misses but dropped 3.2 to 4.1% of in-envelope presses, mostly on the G1 waist and H1 torso, and post hoc none of the dropped cases was out of spec under the old rule. Its "Next" asked for a margin weighted by what a flip costs at the nose.
- **Known before the run (from reading the code, no number computed).** The nose residual along the wall is linear in the joint error (`pose_range.nose_bias`: y = w &middot; &delta; with w the wall row of `backlash.tool_rows` at the nose), and a joint's backlash term is &minus;sign(&tau;<sub>hi</sub>) gap/2 when it is held at both window ends, else 0 (`pose_range.held_bias`). With the settled torque inside [&tau;&#770;<sub>w</sub> &minus; r<sub>j,w</sub>, &tau;&#770;<sub>w</sub> + r<sub>j,w</sub>] at each end w, the set of held states the robot can reach (held +, held &minus;, free) can be listed, so each joint's worst flip cost c<sub>j</sub> = |w<sub>j</sub>| &middot; max |hb &minus; hb&#770;| over that set is exact for independent end intervals and conservative otherwise; the sum &Sigma; c<sub>j</sub> bounds the backlash part of the residual by the triangle inequality. Joints outside their band cost 0, so milestone 55's rule is the special case of a zero budget.
- **Model.** Milestone 55's setup unchanged (its run imported). The weighted rule, from the measured load only: (1) on the pose's sides, choose the squeeze on milestone 38's grid over [&minus;S, S] to minimise the flip bound Y = &Sigma;<sub>j</sub> c<sub>j</sub> (ties: the larger smallest robust arm margin, then the smaller |squeeze|); accept if Y &le; &beta; &middot; 0.03 mm with &beta; = 0.5 (half the spec left for windup and the calibration, whose worst in milestone 55 was 0.011 mm); (2) otherwise, if some leg joint has a non-zero cost, every hold-side combination with its own squeeze, taking the smallest Y among those that pass; (3) otherwise no plan. The calibration plan keeps milestone 55's strict rule (it passed at every error there). Sections A and B: milestone 55's (seed 54 loads on the 6 sensor axes; seed 55 loads with 6 random 0.2 N errors). Section C, fresh: 48 new Latin-hypercube loads (seed 56) with 6 random-direction 0.2 N errors (seed 56).
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H223 (spec).** Under the weighted rule every planned in-envelope pose-case has a residual along the wall of at most 0.03 mm on both robots in all three sections. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H224 (coverage).** Inside milestone 51's envelope the weighted rule plans at least 99% of pose-cases on each robot in every section (milestone 55: 95.9 to 96.8%). Refuted if any robot in any section falls below 99%.
  - **H225 (the bound holds).** At every planned case, the robot's residual minus the residual it would have with the plan's held states (backlash flips removed) is at most the plan's Y, so the bound is never beaten. Refuted at any case where it is (by more than 1e-9 mm).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 55 result: a held-or-free margin covering the 0.2 N sensor error clears milestone 54's spec misses (H220 held) but drops 3.2 to 4.1% of in-envelope cases (H221, H222 refuted), most at the waist, which nothing preloads, and none of the dropped cases was out of spec under the old rule

`experiments/m55_robust/` (run, results, figure), `reproduce.py` (m55 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 2182 s on 4 processes. Two 2-load smoke runs (`M55_N=2`) ran after the hypotheses were committed and before the full run; the second checked a faster replica of the plan against the first and matched it; neither is reported.

- **Checks.** The replica of milestone 53's `plan` (one hold QP and leg-preload solve per window end, then linear settles) matches `plan` to 0.0 N m at 3158 of 3158 cases checked (error 0 of every load and pose), with no plan/no-plan mismatch. The settled torque at &minus;B along x matches &minus;G<sub>x</sub>B to 2.1e-13 N m, so &Delta;&tau; is linear in the load error as the code implied. The sensor-error radius r<sub>j</sub> = B||G<sub>j</sub>|| reaches 0.27 N m (median of each plan's largest 0.21 to 0.22 N m), more than the 0.2 N m friction torque itself: on those joints the margin can only be met by holding them hard, not by leaving them free.
- **Spec (H220 held).** On milestone 54's 48 loads at its 6 sensor axes, every planned in-envelope case is inside spec: worst G1 0.0107 mm, H1 0.0099 mm (milestone 54: 0.284 and 0.042 mm). No planned case on either section has a held or side flip between plan and robot (0 of 18 119), so the backlash part of the error is gone, as the construction says. All 4 of milestone 54's misses now plan in spec: the G1 one on the pose's sides with a re-chosen squeeze (robust margin 0.018 N m, 0.0091 mm), the 3 H1 ones on per-load sides (0.0029 to 0.0058 mm).
- **Coverage (H221 refuted).** Inside milestone 51's envelope the robust rule plans G1 3856 of 4002 (96.4%) and H1 5253 of 5436 (96.6%), against 100% for milestone 54's rule. 434 G1 and 302 H1 cases need per-load sides. The slack calibration passes the rule at every sensor error on both robots.
- **Fresh loads, off-axis errors (H222 refuted).** On 48 new loads (seed 55) with 6 random-direction 0.2 N errors: G1 3762 of 3924 (95.9%), H1 5248 of 5424 (96.8%), worst 0.0110 and 0.0091 mm. Milestone 54's rule on the same loads plans 100% and is also inside spec (worst G1 0.0292 mm, H1 0.0147 mm), so this seed holds no miss for the rule to fix.
- **What binds.** Of the 667 dropped in-envelope cases, the binding joint is a G1 waist joint (yaw, roll or pitch) at 291 of 308 and the H1 torso at 281 of 359; the rest are leg joints (knees mostly) and 5 H1 elbow cases, and 8 have no plan at all on any side. The hold sides move only the ground reaction and so only the leg torques, and the squeeze has zero net wrench, so neither moves a waist torque: those cases were not side-searched. Every one of the 125 cases that was side-searched had 0 of 16 or 64 combinations passing.
- **The rule is conservative (post hoc, not registered).** On the fresh loads, every case the robust rule drops was inside spec under milestone 54's rule (worst G1 0.0110 mm, H1 0.0147 mm, none above 0.02 mm). A waist that the plan holds and the robot frees moves the nose less than the spec here, so asking every joint for the full margin costs 3 to 4% of coverage to fix errors that, on these joints, would not have mattered.
- **What it means.** The fix for milestone 54's misses is a margin on the held-or-free threshold sized from the sensor bound and the settle map, and it works by construction for the backlash term. Applied to every joint it is too strict: the waist and torso, which no preload reaches, sit inside the band about 3% of the time. The rule a robot should run is a margin weighted by what a flip costs at the nose.
- **Not measured.** The time the side search takes on a robot (up to 64 small QPs per press); a sensor error that is not a constant bias; statics only; &mu; 0.5 not measured for these soles.
- **Next.** Weight the margin by the flip's cost: require it only on joints where half a backlash gap, through the wall-direction Jacobian, would move the nose by more than a set share of the spec; or give the waist a preload (a small torso lean or a hold moment through the tool) and re-run both seeds.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 55 planned: does a held-or-free margin that covers the sensor error on every joint, arms included, close milestone 54's spec misses, and what coverage does it cost?

- **Why.** Milestone 54 found 4 of 9438 planned in-envelope pose-cases over the 0.03 mm spec (worst 0.284 mm), each one joint whose planned torque sat a few hundredths of a N m from the 0.2 N m friction torque, so the plan (from the measured load) and the robot (under the true load) disagreed on whether that joint is held. Its "Next" asked for a margin that covers the sensor error on every joint.
- **Known before the run (from reading the code, no number computed).** `preload.settle` is linear in the load (a pseudo-inverse correction), so the change from planned to settled torque is linear in the sensor error: &Delta;&tau;<sub>j</sub> = G<sub>j</sub> &delta; for a load error &delta;. G can be read from three extra calls of milestone 53's `plan` at the same measured load with the truth moved along x, y and z. If every joint's planned |&tau;| at both window ends is further than r<sub>j</sub> = B &middot; ||G<sub>j</sub>|| from the 0.2 N m friction torque (B = 0.2 N, the sensor bound already used), no error in that ball can change any joint's held status or side, so the backlash part of the joint error is zero by construction; the windup part is not touched. What is unknown is how many plans pass, and whether the squeeze and side choice can make the rest pass.
- **Model.** Milestone 54's setup unchanged (its run imported: milestone 48's sides, 'braked' gaps, |e| 0.2 N, S 10 N, slack calibration, per-load side rule, the drag envelope bisected at the sampled direction). The robust rule, run from the measured load only: (1) plan on the pose's sides and re-choose the squeeze on milestone 38's grid over [&minus;S, S] to maximise the smallest robust margin ||&tau;<sub>j</sub>| &minus; 0.2| &minus; r<sub>j</sub> over the arm joints (inside each joint's torque limit, as milestone 38 does); accept if the robust margin is non-negative on every actuated joint at both window ends; (2) otherwise search every hold-side combination (64 G1, 16 H1) with the same squeeze step and take the one with the largest smallest robust margin among those that pass; (3) otherwise no plan. The calibration plan (slack hose) is held to the same rule. Section A: milestone 54's 48 loads (seed 54) at its 6 sensor axes. Section B, a fresh test: 48 new Latin-hypercube loads (seed 55) with 6 sensor errors of 0.2 N in random directions (seed 55), not on the axes the rule never sees anyway.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H220 (spec, milestone 54's loads).** Under the robust rule every planned in-envelope pose-case has a residual along the wall of at most 0.03 mm on both robots. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H221 (coverage).** Inside milestone 51's envelope the robust rule plans at least 99% of pose-cases on each robot (milestone 54: 100%). Refuted if either robot falls below 99%.
  - **H222 (fresh loads, off-axis errors).** On the seed-55 loads and errors, every planned in-envelope pose-case is inside 0.03 mm and coverage is at least 99% on each robot. Refuted if either fails.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 54 result: at 48 Latin-hypercube hose loads every in-envelope press still has a plan (H217 held), but 4 of 9438 leave the 0.03 mm spec, up to 0.284 mm, in direction windows 1 to 5 degrees wide the 8-direction grid never sampled (H218 refuted); the grid's drag envelope is up to 2.2 N optimistic between directions (H219 refuted)

`experiments/m54_lhs/` (run, results, figure, post hoc), `reproduce.py` (m54 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 461 s on 4 processes (post hoc 99 s). A 2-load smoke run (`M54_N=2`) ran after the hypotheses were committed and before the full run; it is not reported.

- **Reproduction.** The bisection run at the 8 grid directions gives milestone 50's limits exactly (largest difference 0.00 N, no None mismatch), so the sampled limits come from the same test.
- **The grid envelope between directions (H219 refuted).** At 21 (pose, direction) pairs the drag limit at the sampled direction is more than 0.5 N below the smaller of its two grid neighbours, all on the G1 at the 0.4 to 0.8 m grips with the hose pulling back and sideways (186 to 250 degrees); the largest shortfall is 2.17 N ((0.4 m, &minus;0.15) at 202.6 degrees: 26.44 N against 28.61 N). None matters at the drags swept here: every dip is at a limit of 23 N or more, above the 20 N the loads reach, and the poses whose limits bind (G1 1.2 m, 4.0 to 4.2 N; H1 1.6 m, 12.0 N) match the grid within 0.05 N, because their weakest direction is straight back, a grid direction. The H1 has no dip over 0.5 N.
- **Coverage (H217 held).** Inside milestone 51's envelope at the sampled directions the G1 plans 4002 of 4002 pose-cases (3943 on the pose's hold sides, 59 by per-load sides) and the H1 5436 of 5436 (5379 and 57). Inside the pose-only envelope: G1 4056 of 4056, H1 5436 of 5436. Of the 300 pose-cases past their pose's limit (G1 264, H1 36) none plans on the pose's sides (they were not side-searched), as milestone 51 found on the grid. The slack calibration planned at all 288 (load, sensor axis) pairs.
- **Accuracy (H218 refuted).** 4 planned in-envelope pose-cases exceed 0.03 mm: G1 (0.4 m, &minus;0.15) at 13.23 N, 290.2 degrees, sensor axis 2: **0.284 mm**; H1 (1.2 m, &minus;0.15) at 4.75 N, 305.5 degrees: 0.042 and 0.034 mm (axes 0 and 2); H1 (1.2 m, &minus;0.15) at 12.62 N, 210.5 degrees: 0.032 mm. All 4 are planned on the pose's own sides. Worst by drag band, G1 0.016 / 0.023 / 0.284 / 0.011 mm and H1 0.042 / 0.013 / 0.032 / 0.015 mm over [0, 5), [5, 10), [10, 15), [15, 20] N.
- **Why (post hoc, not registered; `posthoc.txt`).** Each excess is one joint whose torque at one window end sits at the 0.2 N m friction torque at which the model counts a joint as held on one side of its backlash, so the plan (made from the measured load) and the robot (under the true load) disagree on whether it is held, and the error is half a backlash gap (7.3e-4 to 7.5e-4 rad on that joint, every other joint unchanged). G1: the right shoulder yaw, planned &minus;0.226 N m at 50 N, settled &minus;0.200 N m (the arm squeeze is &minus;0.05 N). H1: the left hip pitch (planned &minus;0.100 N m at 25 N, settled &minus;0.273) and the left knee (planned +0.300 at 50 N, settled +0.151). A &plusmn;6 degree sweep at 0.25 degree steps puts the over-spec points inside spans 2.75 to 4.75 degrees wide (the G1's is broken: 10 of the 16 points in its 3.75 degree span; 0.009 mm outside it); with grid directions 45 degrees apart, a window that narrow is easy to miss. Applying milestone 53's per-load side choice anyway brings all 3 H1 cases inside spec (0.0011 to 0.0058 mm, 3.8 to 5.6 N m of leg margin), but not the G1 one (0.284 mm unchanged: the joint is an arm joint, not a hold or leg joint).
- **What it means.** Coverage survives the move off the grid; the spec does not, quite. The failure is not a plan limit but a threshold the plan sits on: the leg preload asks a margin over the friction torque, but the plan uses the measured load, so a joint the plan puts a few hundredths of a N m past the threshold can land on the other side under the true load. On the joints that tip over, planned and settled torque differ by 0.026 to 0.173 N m, more than the distance from the threshold the plan left them.
- **Not measured.** How often these windows occur over a continuous load distribution (48 loads give 4 of 9438 in-envelope cases, 0.04%, an estimate with wide error); whether the H1 dips past 0.5 N at directions not sampled. Statics only; constant sensor bias; μ 0.5 not measured.
- **Next.** Ask the plan for a held-or-free margin that covers the sensor error on every joint, arms included: no joint's planned torque within |&Delta;&tau;(e)| of the 0.2 N m threshold at either window end (or the squeeze and side choice that keeps it out), then rerun these 48 loads and a fresh LHS seed.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 54 planned: off the 8-direction grid, do the three hose rules still give every in-envelope press a plan inside spec, and is the grid's drag envelope safe between its directions?

- **Why.** Milestones 50 to 53 sampled hose drag at 0, 5 and 20 N in 8 directions 45 degrees apart, and the drag limit each pose tolerates (milestone 50) was bisected at those 8 directions only. Milestone 53's "Next" asked to drop the grid: sample loads continuously inside the envelope and check the three rules (slack calibration, each press inside its pose's drag limit, hold sides chosen per measured load) hold off the 8 directions.
- **Known before the run.** Nothing below has been computed. The envelope between grid directions is unknown: the tolerated drag could dip between two grid directions, so an envelope read off the grid could admit loads no plan exists for.
- **Model.** Milestone 53's setup unchanged (its run imported: milestone 48's sides, 'braked' gaps, |e| 0.2 N on the 6 sensor axes, S 10 N, slack calibration, per-load side rule). Hose loads: 48 Latin-hypercube points over drag H in [0, 20] N (the swept range) and direction &phi; in [0, 360) degrees, seed 54, the same 48 for both robots, each at the 6 sensor axes and every pose (G1 15, H1 19: 4320 and 5472 pose-cases). At each sampled &phi; and each pose (and the calibration pose) the drag limit is bisected exactly as milestone 50 did (largest H in [0, 60] N at which both 25 and 50 N balance, current lean, 25 steps). Envelope: milestone 51's (H at most the limit at the pose and at the calibration pose, at the sampled &phi;), and the pose-only one (the pose's limit only). Rule: plan on the pose's sides; if none, milestone 53's per-load side search. Residual along the wall with the slack calibration.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H217 (coverage).** Inside milestone 51's envelope evaluated at the sampled directions, the three rules give a plan at every LHS pose-case on both robots. Refuted if any in-envelope pose-case has none.
  - **H218 (spec).** Every planned in-envelope LHS pose-case has a residual along the wall of at most 0.03 mm. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H219 (grid envelope is safe).** At every sampled direction and pose, the drag limit bisected there is at least the smaller of the limits at the two neighbouring grid directions minus 0.5 N (so an envelope read off the grid by taking the smaller neighbour never admits a load more than 0.5 N past the true limit). Refuted if any pose at any sampled direction falls more than 0.5 N below.

`experiments/m53_loadside/` (run, results, figure; imports milestone 52's run, no change to it), `reproduce.py` (m53 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 117 s on 4 processes.

- **The leg preload binds, not the hold QP (H214 refuted).** At 20 of the 26 plan-limit pose-cases the first stage that fails is the leg-preload solve: the hold QP solves on the pose's sides, but the largest common leg margin it allows (`margin_lp`) is below the 0.1 N m asked (G1 0.004 to 0.092 N m; H1 0.019 to 0.062 N m against its tiered 0.0864 / 0.1 N m). The binding leg joints at the optimum are ankle rolls and hip rolls and yaws: G1 (1.2 m, 0) at 20 N φ 45 the left ankle roll at the 25 N end (6 of 6), (1.0 m, 0) at 20 N φ 90 the right ankle roll at the 50 N end (3 of 3). Only 6 fail at the hold QP, all at (1.0 m, +0.15) at 20 N φ 225 at the 50 N end, where 32 or 33 of the 64 side combinations still have a hold solution. Of the 7 sensor-error cases, 5 fail at the preload and 2 at the hold QP.
- **Per-load sides clear every case inside milestone 51's envelope.** The rule (keep the pose's sides when they plan, else take the side combination with the largest common leg margin at the measured load among those whose full plan solves) recovers 31 of the 33 drops. Between 8 (H1, of 16) and 52 (G1, of 64) combinations plan at each case, and the chosen one's common leg margin is 1.84 to 5.22 N m on the G1 and 2.47 to 3.27 N m on the H1, against 0.1 N m asked. Most G1 recoveries flip two joints of milestone 48's sides, the left hip yaw and left ankle roll (`--++-+` to `-+-+-+`, 15 of 26); the H1 flips its right hip roll (4 of 5).
- **Coverage.** Inside milestone 51's envelope the G1 now plans 1368 of 1368 pose-cases (100%, from 98.1%) and the H1 1902 of 1902 (100%, from 99.7%).
- **In spec (H216 held).** Every recovered case's residual along the wall is at most 0.0106 mm (H1 (1.6 m, 0) at 5 N straight back), so each robot's worst inside the envelope is unchanged: G1 0.016 / 0.011 / 0.011 mm, H1 0.010 / 0.014 / 0.010 mm at 0 / 5 / 20 N.
- **What is left (H215 refuted).** The 2 G1 sensor-error cases at (0.8 m, &minus;0.15) at 20 N φ 180, which are inside only the pose-only envelope (the drag passes the calibration pose's limit, so they need the slack calibration to count at all): at the 50 N end no side combination of 64 has a hold solution, so no side choice can help there (a hold-QP limit at that measured load; why it fails was not examined).
- **What it means.** With the three rules together (calibrate with the hose slack; keep each press inside its pose's drag limit; pick the hold sides at each press from the measured load), every swept case inside the drag envelope has a plan on both robots and stays inside the 0.03 mm spec. The per-pose side choice of milestones 46 to 48 was the right idea at too coarse a grain: one side set per pose cannot hold under every hose load, but one per measured load can.
- **Not measured.** Whether switching hold sides between presses costs time or wear (each switch moves the joints across their backlash); the side search is 16 to 64 small QPs per press, run offline here. Statics only; μ 0.5 not measured for these soles; constant sensor bias only.
- **Next.** Drop the sweep's grid: sample hose loads continuously inside the envelope (Latin hypercube over drag and direction) and check the three rules hold off the 8 directions; then the cost of side switches (backlash crossings per wall).

- **The slack calibration always plans (H213 held).** Under the hanging hose's weight alone the calibration pose plans at all 6 sensor-error axes on both robots, so no case is dropped by the calibration.

The H1 is unchanged: 1897 of 1902 (99.7%). Inside a pose-only envelope (the calibration no longer carries drag, so only the pose's own limit applies) the G1 plans 1394 of 1422 (98.0%).
- **No residual changes (H212 held).** Over every pose-case that plans under both calibrations, |y| differs by less than 5e-5 mm (prints as 0.0000), and the worst residuals are milestone 51's: G1 0.016 / 0.011 / 0.011 mm, H1 0.010 / 0.014 / 0.010 mm at 0 / 5 / 20 N. Why, checked after the run: `wrist_ft.joint_error` is actual minus predicted error, and the compensator predicts from the measured load, so with braked gaps and linear windup the error left at the calibration pose depends on the sensor error e only. At the G1's calibration pose the slack and 5 N φ 90 calibration errors differ by 4e-19 rad on joints whose error is about 1e-5 rad.

H1: 5 plan limits at (1.6 m, 0) at 5 N straight back. Two more G1 sensor-error cases at (0.8 m, &minus;0.15) 20 N φ 180 lie only in the pose-only envelope.
- **What it means.** The hose rule for both robots is: calibrate once with the hose slack, then keep each press inside its pose's drag limit (milestone 50). In this model that costs no accuracy, because the calibration's job is the sensor bias, not the load.

What they leave is a planning limit, not a balance limit, and most of it sits at one pose, the G1's calibration pose under sideways drag, because one calibration serves every pose. A hose rule for the G1 needs the calibration taken with the hose slack (or at a pose whose plan holds sideways drag) as well as the per-pose drag limits.

`experiments/m50_relean/` (run, results, post hoc, figure; imports milestone 49's run, no change to it), `reproduce.py` (m50 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 152 s on 4 processes.

- **A bug caught before reading results.** The first run's section A tested "balances" as "milestone 49's `cone_slack` returns a value"; that LP returns a negative slack, not None, when the cones cannot hold, so every pose read as tolerating 60 N and a 200 N press. Section A now uses milestone 49's balance-and-cones feasibility LP (as its post hoc did). Sections B to D did not use that test and printed the same numbers in both runs.
- **The drag each pose tolerates (section A, current lean).** The largest hose tension at which 25 and 50 N both balance, per direction: the G1 at 1.2 m grips tolerates **4.0 to 4.2 N straight back** (6.3 to 6.6 N at φ 135 and 225), the G1 at 1.0 m 12.9 to 17.0 N, the H1 at 1.6 m 12.0 N. Every one of the 26 poses that had a side plan in milestone 48 tolerates at least 20.1 N in every direction, so a 20 N sweep sat just inside them. Forward drag (φ 0) is the next limit: 21.5 to 22.5 N at the G1's 0.8 to 1.2 m grips.
- **The rule is linear (H206 held).** At φ 180 the tolerated drag is within 9% of (press top with a slack hose &minus; 50 N) / 0.78 at all 8 poses (1 to 9%). The press top here includes the hanging hose's weight (H 0), so it reads 52.9 N where milestone 49's no-hose figure was 51.6 N.
- **No lean covers the firing window under drag (H204 refuted).** On a 5 mm grid of com_dx from 0 to 150 mm and a ternary refinement, the worst friction-cone slack over the 17 loads at 25 and 50 N peaks below zero at every pose: **&minus;0.69 N** (G1 1.0 m, dy 0 and +0.15, com_dx 90.7 mm), &minus;2.38 N (G1 1.0 m, &minus;0.15), &minus;3.47 N (H1 1.6 m, 113 mm), &minus;10.12 N (G1 1.2 m, 110 mm). Leaning forward gains backward-drag headroom at about 0.53 N of slack per mm, and past the peak a 20 N hose pulling towards the wall at the 25 N press takes over (all three robots and grips): the robot tips forward.
- **Standing at zero press as well is further out (H205 refuted).** With 0 N added to the window, the best worst slack is &minus;19.61 N (G1 1.0 m), &minus;22.18 N (H1), &minus;32.34 N (G1 1.2 m): milestone 41's zero-press stance and a 20 N hose cannot coexist at these grips.
- **H207 not tested.** It was conditional on a lean from H204.
- **At the best lean (section B2)** the smallest tolerated tension over the 8 directions is 14.9 to 19.5 N (G1 1.0 m), 11.4 to 13.4 N (G1 1.2 m), 17.0 N (H1 1.6 m): the lean roughly triples the G1 1.2 m figure but stays under 20 N.
- **As a hose length (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`, after the run, not registered).** A dragged hose holds at most μ_f w g L of tension (`hose.max_drag`). For the rubber 3/8 in hose (0.274 kg/m) at μ_f 0.5, the G1's 1.2 m grips at the current lean reach their limit with **3.0 to 3.1 m** of hose dragging; at the best lean 8.5 to 10.0 m; the H1 at 1.6 m 8.9 m (current) and 12.7 m (best). The hybrid hose roughly doubles each length. μ_f is milestone 37's unsourced sweep (0.3 / 0.5 / 0.8).
- **What it means.** The eight poses' missing plans are not a lean to tune: no lean holds a 20 N drag in every direction, because what a forward lean gives to backward drag it takes from forward drag. The fix is in the hose: at the G1's 1.2 m grips keep the dragged length under about 3 m of rubber hose (or feed slack, a boom or an overhead reel), or lean per load direction if the controller knows it. The residual tables' G1 coverage caveat stands.
- **Not measured.** μ_f for any hose on OSB or slab; dynamic loads when the hose snags; leaning per load direction (needs the direction, which milestone 35 says no controller knows); the side search at a re-lean (H207); statics only, μ 0.5 not measured for these soles.
- **Next.** A hose-management rule in the plan: per pose, the dragged length allowed (or a reel tension set point), and section D's residual sweep restricted to the loads each pose tolerates, with coverage reported; or a lean chosen per hose direction from a direction estimate (the wrist F/T sensor sees the hose force), with its sensing error.

`experiments/m49_backload/` (run, results, post hoc, figure; imports milestone 48's run, no change to it), `reproduce.py` (m49 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests. Run time 105 s on 4 processes (a first attempt lost 20 min to BLAS threads oversubscribing the pool: numpy was imported before milestone 48's `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1` default; the script now sets it first).

- **A correction to milestone 48's post hoc.** Its table 1 prints, per load, "hold solutions / margin LP solutions" at the 25 N end, then at the 50 N end. The entry above read the 25 N pair (e.g. 48/48) as the hold QP solving and the "0/0" as the margin LP failing. In fact both solvers agree everywhere: they both solve at the 25 N end and neither has a solution at the 50 N end. There was no disagreement to explain.
- **No balancing force at 50 N (H199 refuted).** At the 50 N end of all 24 cells, no contact force balances the base inside the μ 0.5 friction cones: the best cone slack is &minus;23.77 to &minus;0.10 N. At the 25 N end all 24 balance (slack at least 6.98 N).
- **No false negative (H200 refuted).** The hold QP has no solution at any 50 N cell, so there is no hold force to test. At the 25 N end the margin LP solves at all 984 hold solutions, which have base residual at most 1.7e-13 and cone violation at most 2.0e-9. Section B (a fallback for false negatives, then a rerun) was conditional on one, so **H201 to H203 were not tested**: the dropped section D cases are real stance limits, and milestone 48's coverage caveat stands.
- **What limits the stance (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`, after section A, not registered).** The net horizontal load the soles carry is at most 0.189 of the vertical load (G1; H1 0.127), well inside μ 0.5, so the stance does not slide as a whole. 13 of the G1's 18 cells do not balance with unlimited friction either: the press plus a backward hose pulls the centre of pressure off the heels, so the robot tips back. 3 G1 cells with a diagonal or sideways hose balance at μ 0.503 to 0.563, just above 0.5 (yaw friction). The other 2 G1 cells and all 6 H1 cells balance only with unbounded friction (none up to μ 5), which is also a centre-of-pressure limit for practical purposes.
- **The press window shrinks under drag.** Bisecting the largest press that balances at μ 0.5: G1 51.6 to 62.2 N with no hose, 36.4 to 49.8 N with the cell's drag; H1 58.7 N, 42.8 to 48.6 N. Straight back at 20 N (φ 180) the top falls by 15.2 N (G1 1.2 m grips, 51.6 to 36.4 N), 14.8 N (G1 1.0 m, 62.2 to 47.4) and 15.9 N (H1 1.6 m, 58.7 to 42.8): about 0.75 to 0.8 N of press per newton of backward drag. Milestone 32 leaned each pose to cover [25, 50] N with no hose, and at these grips the top had only 1.6 to 12 N to spare.
- **What it means.** The poses milestone 48 could not plan are not a numerical artefact to fix in the solver; they are poses whose lean leaves less headroom above 50 N than a backward hose takes away. The fix is in the pose or the hose: lean those poses forward so the window covers [25, 50] N plus the drag, or route the hose so it does not pull backward at the high grips.
- **Not measured.** The bottom of the press window under drag (a forward lean costs headroom there); the G1's calibration pose, whose drops come from the same check but were not diagnosed here; statics only; μ 0.5 not measured for these soles on OSB or slab.
- **Next.** Re-lean the 8 poses (milestone 32's lean search) for the window [25, 50] N under the worst backward drag, check the 25 N end still balances at the worst forward drag, and rerun section D with the coverage reported; or find the largest backward drag each pose tolerates, as a hose-routing rule.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 49 planned: is the backward-hose infeasibility a stance limit or a solver false negative?

- **Why.** Milestone 48's post hoc found 24 (pose, hose load) cells, all at the 50 N press end and mostly 20 N of drag pulling backward, where the 'hold' QP returns a solution for 37 to 56 of the G1's 64 side combinations (15 to 16 of the H1's 16) but the preload margin LP (`preload.margin_lp`) has none for any. The plan path (`preload.solve_margin`) calls the same LP, so section D drops those cases: 258 of the G1's 1530 pose-cases (17%), 41 of the H1's 1938. Its "Next" asked which it is.
- **What is known before the run (from reading the code, no number computed).** `grf_qp.solve('hold')` returns a force only after `grf_qp.margin_lp` (balance plus friction cones plus the hold rows) is feasible, and falls back to that LP's point when SLSQP's is not clean, so the stance can balance at those cells. `preload.margin_lp` adds only rows `t <= kappa tau + d` with `t` free in [&minus;1000, 1000] N m, which a balancing force satisfies unless some leg torque passes 1000 N m. So a true infeasibility there is unexpected; nothing here has been run to check it.
- **Model.** Milestone 48's import and sides. Section A, at every cell of milestone 48's post hoc table 1 and both window ends, for every side combination the hold QP solves: the hold force's base residual and cone violation, `preload.margin_lp`'s HiGHS status and message, the common leg margin evaluated at the hold force itself (an explicit feasible point of the margin LP when it is finite), and a re-solve of the same LP (scaled rows, `t` bound widened). Section B, if section A finds a false negative, `preload.margin_lp` patched to fall back on the best explicit point (never a looser constraint), then milestone 48's side search and section D rerun on both robots with the drop counts beside the worst residuals.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H199 (the stance balances).** At every one of the 24 cells, a balance-and-cones LP has a solution. Refuted if any lacks one. (A check: the hold QP needed it.)
  - **H200 (a false negative).** At every cell, for at least one side combination, the hold QP's force has base residual and cone violation below 1e-6 and a finite common leg margin, so the margin LP was feasible. Refuted if any cell has none.
  - **H201 (coverage returns).** With the fix, the G1 drops under 5% of its swept pose-cases (milestone 48: 17%) and the H1 none (2%). Refuted otherwise.
  - **H202 (the G1 stays in spec on the full sweep).** The G1's worst residual along the wall, over the recovered sweep, is at most 0.03 mm at 0, 5 and 20 N of drag. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H203 (the H1 stays in spec on the full sweep).** The same for the H1. Refuted if any is larger.

- **Model.** Milestone 47's import (so M1 is the object milestones 41 to 47 built), with its side rule replaced. At every pose of both robots in milestone 41's set, S_H is the combination (16 for the H1, 64 for the G1) whose smallest common leg margin over both window ends and all 17 hose loads (0 N, and 5 and 20 N in each of 8 directions, the same loads section D sweeps), with an exact sensor, is largest; ties (1e-9 N m) keep the per-robot side. "Ties keep the per-robot side" would then pick by enumeration order. Ties (within 1e-9 N m) now go to the larger zero-force margin (milestone 47's score), then to the per-robot side.

No new library code, so no new tests.

- **The rule.** At each pose, S* is the hold-side combination (16 for the H1, 64 for the G1) with the largest common leg margin at zero hose force and an exact sensor, the smaller window end. It differs from the per-robot side at every pose of both robots (15 of 15 G1, 19 of 19 H1), though at several G1 poses only by a tie-level amount (G1 (0.6 m, 0): 3.6116 to 3.6128 N m).
- **Every pose takes the spec preload (H187 held).** Smallest S* margin 0.886 N m (H1 (1.4 m, 0)), against the 0.111 goal; the H1's margins rise from 0.033–4.21 to 0.886–12.79 N m, the G1's from 0.141–4.69 to 0.975–5.31. Milestone 45's tier is no longer needed anywhere.
- **The two skipped H1 poses gain a plan (H188 held).** (0.4 m, −0.15 m) and (1.6 m, −0.15 m), which milestone 38 skipped for having no 'hold-min' solution on the per-robot sides, have S* margins of 12.79 and 6.62 N m. They are not in the swept set yet: adding them needs milestone 38's pose set rebuilt.
- **The H1 improves (H189, H192 held).** 'braked', |e| 0.2 N, S 10 N: the H1's worst residual along the wall is 0.010 / 0.014 / **0.010 mm** at 0 / 5 / 20 N of hose drag (milestone 46: 0.010 / 0.019 / 0.032). The swept cases with no plan at milestone 44's two poses fall from 23 to 18 of 204 (none at 5 N, from 5).
- **The stack does not move (H191 held).** Every pose's spec stack is within 0.01 points of milestone 46's; worst 'braked' G1 0.9257, H1 0.9655, unchanged.
- **The G1 gets worse under hose drag (H190 refuted).** The G1's worst residual is 0.011 / 0.025 / **0.037 mm** at 0 / 5 / 20 N (milestone 41: 0.016 / 0.011 / 0.011), over the 0.03 mm spec at 20 N. The post hoc (`posthoc.txt`, after the run) checks the harness first (the G1 on its per-robot sides reproduces milestone 41 to 0.000 mm) and finds the rise at three of 15 poses, each with one leg joint (not a hold-set joint) flipping at the worst case: (0.6 m, −0.15 m) 0.006 → 0.037 mm at 20 N, (1.2 m, 0.15 m) 0.006 → 0.025 at 5 N and → 0.016 at 20 N, (1.2 m, 0) 0.001 → 0.004 at 20 N. One G1 pose improves (1.2 m, 0.15 m) at 0 N: 0.016 → 0.006.
- **What it means.** A per-pose side is the right idea for the H1: it rescues two poses, lifts every margin and cuts its worst residual by two thirds at 20 N. But a margin measured at zero hose force is the wrong score to pick sides by: on the G1, sides that widen the zero-drag margin narrow some leg's margin under drag. The controller should score sides at the loads it will meet (the hose sweep), or keep the per-robot side where the gain is small.
- **Not measured.** Statics; mu 0.5 and the 'braked' waist; sides scored under hose drag or sensor error; the two rescued H1 poses in the sweep; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.
- **Next.** Score hold sides by the worst-case margin over the hose and sensor sweep (or the worst residual), per pose, for both robots; rebuild the pose set with the two rescued H1 poses and sweep them.

It is `right_hip_roll`, in milestone 32's hold set and one of the leg rows milestone 37's preload acts on: planned at 0.2300 N m (τ_f + 0.030) at both window ends, it settles to 0.1974 under a (0, 0, −0.2) N sensor error. The preload reached it; the margin was too small.
- **Check.** The other 17 H1 poses reproduce milestone 41's worst residuals (0.010 / 0.019 / 0.032 mm) to 0.000 mm; the tiered poses are exactly milestone 44's two.
- **No room on the hold set (H175 refuted).** With every other leg row allowed down to its threshold, the largest common margin on the hold-set rows is T_H = **0.0339 N m** at (1.4 m, −0.15 m) (binding: right hip roll, 50 N end), against the common 0.0333: the other legs were not what capped it. At (1.6 m, 0) T_H is 0.0960 against 0.0946 (left hip roll). The other leg rows, by contrast, had room: given the hold set at m_H, T_L is 0.190 and 0.656 N m, so they take the full 0.1 N m.
- **The plan survives, the stack does not move (H176, H179, H180 held).** Both poses plan; stacks 0.9715 and 0.9668 as milestone 44 (+0.00 points); 24 of 204 swept cases at the two poses have no plan, as in milestone 44.
- **The residual does not come back (H177, H178 refuted).** 'braked', |e| 0.2 N: (1.4 m, −0.15 m) leaves **0.172 mm** at 0, 5 and 20 N drag (one hold-set flip at each worst case). The 20 N figure falls from milestone 44's 0.241 to 0.172 (the other leg rows now carry 0.1 N m), so the H1's worst over all poses is 0.172 mm at every drag. (1.6 m, 0) stays at 0.003–0.014 mm.
- **What it needs instead (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`).** The right hip roll moves **0.163 N m per newton** of sensor error along z (0.016 along x; a 1 N error along y leaves no plan, so that axis has no figure) at both window ends. So the pose holds the spec only while 0.163 |e_z| stays under the hold-set margin: with m_H at 0.9 T_H (0.0305) that is |e_z| < 0.19 N, and the post hoc finds 0.001–0.004 mm at |e| 0.05–0.15 N and 0.172 at 0.2 N; with m_H at 0.99 T_H (0.0336) |e| 0.2 N leaves 0.005 mm, a back-off of 1% that no real controller would trust. This pose is decided by the wrist sensor's z accuracy (0.2 N is two FT 300 standard deviations, milestone 36), not by any preload the stance allows: an ATI Mini45-class error (2 × 1/16 N = 0.125 N, milestone 36's sweep) passes, an FT 300's 0.2 N does not.
- **What it means.** The zero-press stance keeps a spec-grade plan at 4 of the H1's 6 re-leaned poses at |e| 0.2 N, and at 5 of 6 with a sensor whose z error stays under about 0.19 N; a designer who cannot promise that should keep milestone 32's lean at (1.4 m, −0.15 m). A hold-first preload is still worth having elsewhere: it costs no stack and gave the other leg rows three to six times more margin than the common rule.
- **Not measured.** Statics; mu 0.5 and the 'braked' waist; a different hold set (choosing which roll joint to hold at this pose); the G1 (untouched, its smallest common margin 0.141 N m).
- **Next.** The tool-point spec as one table over both robots and both leans, now with the sensor bound per pose; whether re-choosing the hold set at (1.4 m, −0.15 m) frees the right hip roll; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 45 planned: a hold-first (two-tier) preload for H1 (1.4 m, −0.15 m)

- **Why.** Milestone 44's per-pose leg preload left a 0.172 mm residual at H1 (1.4 m, −0.15 m), and its post hoc said the joint that flips there is "a held joint the preload does not reach". A diagnostic before this plan (worst case of milestone 44's no-drag sweep, β 0.9, sensor error (0, 0, −0.2) N) shows that is wrong: the joint is `right_hip_roll`, one of milestone 32's hold set, which *is* one of the leg rows milestone 37's preload acts on (the hold set is a subset of the legs). Its planned torque is 0.2300 N m at both window ends, exactly τ_f + m_k (0.2 + 0.030), and under the true load it settles to 0.1974 N m, 0.0026 below τ_f: a 0.2 N z error moves it 0.033 N m, more than the 0.030 margin. So the preload reached it; the common margin the stance allows every leg row (0.033 N m) is too small. The open question is whether the stance can give the hold-set rows a larger margin if the other leg rows take less.
- **Model.** Milestone 44's import (so M1 is the object milestones 41 to 44 built; its per-pose preload is switched off, so every pose plans with 0.1 N m unless listed here). New library code `src/buildbot_sheathing/tiered.py` (`tier_lp`: the largest common margin on a chosen subset of rows with every other row at or above its own floor; `solve_tiered`: the controller QP with a per-row margin target), with tests. Per pose whose common margin t_min is below 0.1/β (milestone 44: exactly the two poses), at zero hose force and an exact sensor, at both window ends: T_H = the largest common margin on the hold-set rows with the other leg rows at margin ≥ 0; m_H = min(0.1, β T_H); then T_L = the largest common margin on the other leg rows with the hold-set rows at ≥ m_H; m_L = min(0.1, β T_L) (no plan if T_L < 0). β = 0.9 as milestone 44 registered. Milestone 38's `plan` is called unchanged except that the preload step solves with target m_H on hold-set rows and m_L on the rest. Sections: A, T_H, m_H, T_L, m_L per pose; B, the spec stack at the two poses ('stock', 'braked') beside milestone 44's; C, the H1's worst spec stack; D, the worst residual along the wall ('braked', |e| 0.2 N, S 10 N, H 0 / 5 / 20 N, every hose direction and sensor axis), all poses, the other poses (check: equal to milestone 41's) and each of the two poses, with cases with no plan and the flipped moving joints at each pose's worst case.
- **Disclosed before registering.** The diagnostic above (one case, β 0.9). No tiered margin, plan or residual has been computed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H175 (the stance has room on the hold set).** At (1.4 m, −0.15 m), T_H ≥ 0.05 N m. Refuted if smaller.
  - **H176 (the plan survives the trade).** Both poses have a tiered spec plan at zero hose force and an exact sensor (T_L ≥ 0). Refuted if either has none.
  - **H177 (the pose is rescued).** 'braked', |e| 0.2 N: the worst residual along the wall at (1.4 m, −0.15 m) is at most 0.03 mm at 0 and 5 N drag and 0.05 mm at 20 N. Refuted if any is larger.
  - **H178 (the H1's tool-point spec holds).** The H1's worst residual over all its poses is at most 0.03 mm at 5 N and 0.05 mm at 20 N (milestone 44's H174, refuted at 0.172 and 0.241). Refuted if either is larger.
  - **H179 (no more lost cases).** The swept cases at the two poses with no plan are at most milestone 44's 24 of 204. Refuted if more.
  - **H180 (the stack does not move).** 'braked': each pose's spec stack is within 0.1 points of milestone 44's (0.9715 and 0.9668). Refuted if further.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 44 result: a per-pose leg preload gives both dropped H1 poses a plan at no clean-sheet cost, but only (1.6 m, 0) meets the tool-point spec; at (1.4 m, −0.15 m) a hold-set joint flips and the residual is 0.17 mm; H170, H172 and H173 held, H171 and H174 refuted

`experiments/m44_legpreload/` (run, results, post hoc, figure; imports milestone 43's run, so M1 is the object milestones 41 to 43 built, and calls milestone 38's `plan` unchanged with a per-pose preload), `reproduce.py` (m44 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests.

- **Check.** The other 17 H1 poses keep 0.1 N m and reproduce milestone 41's H1 worst residuals (0.010 / 0.019 / 0.032 mm at 0 / 5 / 20 N) exactly; the rule changes exactly milestone 43's two dropped poses (t_min 0.0333 and 0.0946 N m, so m_k 0.0300 and 0.0851).
- **The plan returns, and the stack does not care how big the preload is (H170, H172, H173 held).** With β = 0.9 both poses have a spec plan, squeeze at its −10 N bound. Their stacks are 0.9715 and 0.9668 at every β in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.9}, 0.08 and 0.27 points under milestone 40's old-lean spec stacks; the H1's worst over its 19 poses stays 0.9655 at (1.6 m, +0.15 m). The stack counts loose joints, and a leg row met at margin 0 holds the joint as surely as one met at 0.1 N m.
- **But the sweep breaks it (H171, H174 refuted).** At |e| 0.2 N, 24 of the 204 swept (H, φ, e) cases at the two poses have no plan (0 of 12 at no drag, 5 of 96 at 5 N, 19 of 96 at 20 N). The H1's worst residual along the wall rises from 0.019 to **0.172 mm** at 5 N drag and from 0.032 to **0.241 mm** at 20 N, all of it at (1.4 m, −0.15 m); at no drag it is 0.172 mm already.
- **Why (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`).** Per pose and β, at the worst case: at (1.6 m, 0) β ≥ 0.5 leaves **0.003 mm** at no drag and 0.014 mm at 5 N, inside the spec, with no hold-set joint flipping (0.46–0.71 mm at β = 0, where two or three do). At (1.4 m, −0.15 m) one joint of milestone 32's hold set still flips at β = 0.5 and 0.9 and the residual stays 0.172 mm (0.35 mm at β ≤ 0.25, two flips). The leg preload acts on the leg rows; the joint that crosses its friction band under a 0.2 N sensor error there is a held joint the preload does not reach, so no preload the stance allows fixes it. A larger preload also costs cases: at 5 N drag (1.6 m, 0) keeps a plan in 46 of 48 cases at β 0.5 and 43 of 48 at β 0.9.
- **What it means.** The per-pose preload rescues (1.6 m, 0), so the zero-press stance keeps a spec-grade plan at 4 of the H1's 6 re-leaned poses instead of 3. At (1.4 m, −0.15 m) it gives a plan but a residual nine times the spec's, still 18 times inside the ±3.14 mm joint-nail window but not the 0.01–0.02 mm figure the paper quotes. There the designer should keep milestone 32's lean and catch the lift-off another way, or give up the pose.
- **Not measured.** Statics; mu 0.5 and the 'braked' waist as before; a preload on the hold-set joints (the QP's hold signs) rather than the legs; the G1 is untouched (its smallest margin, 0.141 N m, carries 0.1).
- **Next.** The tool-point spec as one table over both robots and both leans, with the pose each figure comes from; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses; whether a margin row on the hold-set joint rescues (1.4 m, −0.15 m).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 43 result: in the spec's own convention the zero-press stance gains the G1 0.97 points at its worst pose instead of losing 1.3; the cost moves to the H1, which keeps a spec plan at only 3 of its 6 re-leaned poses; H166, H167 and H168 held, H165 and H169 refuted

`experiments/m43_specstack/` (run, results, post hoc, figure; imports milestone 42's run, so both setups are the objects milestones 41 and 42 built), `reproduce.py` (m43 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card. No new library code, so no new tests.

- **Checks.** Milestone 32's leans reproduce milestone 40's section D stacks to 0.0e+00, and the 'hold-min' torques on the re-leans reproduce milestone 42's 'braked' cells to 0.0e+00.
- **The conventions part at the re-leaned worst pose (H165 refuted).** At the G1's (1.2 m, 0), 'braked', the spec plan (leg preload 0.1 N m, squeeze −4.00 N) gives **0.9300**; 'hold-min' gives 0.9030. They agreed at 0.9160 on milestone 32's lean, so milestone 42's post hoc finding that the conventions agree at each worst pose does not carry over to the new poses: they differ by 2.70 points there.
- **The G1 gains (H166 and H168 held, and more).** Over the whole set the G1's worst spec stack rises from 0.9160 to **0.9257** ('braked', +0.97 points; now at (1.2 m, −0.15 m)) and from 0.9081 to 0.9257 ('stock', +1.76). The comparison is like for like: all 14 poses milestone 40 could plan are planned again, plus (1.0 m, +0.15 m), which had no spec plan on its old lean and now has one (0.9547). Per re-leaned pose ('braked'): −0.31, +0.79, new, −1.01, +1.40, −1.17 points.
- **The H1 pays in poses, not points (H167 held, H169 refuted).** Of the six re-leaned H1 poses, (1.6 m, −0.15 m) lost its controller at milestone 41, and two more, (1.4 m, −0.15 m) and (1.6 m, 0), keep a 'hold-min' solution but have no spec plan. The three that keep one lose 0.09–0.22 points; like for like over the 17 poses both leans can plan, the H1's worst falls from 0.9677 to 0.9655. (Its worst over the set "rises" from 0.9564 to 0.9655 only because 0.9564's pose dropped out: not a gain.)
- **Why the two H1 poses drop (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`).** The hold QP solves at both window ends; what fails is the leg preload at 50 N: the largest leg margin the stance allows is **0.033 N m** at (1.4 m, −0.15 m) and **0.095 N m** at (1.6 m, 0), against milestone 37's 0.1 N m. The same check explains the G1's new pose: at (1.0 m, +0.15 m) the old lean allowed 0.088 N m at 50 N, the re-lean 0.707.
- **What it means.** Milestones 41 and 42's "the zero-press stance costs the G1 1.3 points" is true of the 'hold-min' torques but not of the spec, whose preload and squeeze are re-planned for the new stance and come out ahead on the G1. The real price of the zero-press stance is on the H1 near the top of its reach: half its re-leaned poses cannot carry the 0.1 N m leg preload at a 50 N press. A designer can lower the preload there (both margins are positive), cap the press, or keep the old lean and catch the lift-off another way.
- **Not measured.** Statics; zero hose force and an exact sensor (the residuals along the wall were milestone 41's); the 'set' column of milestone 40 (the re-press) is not recomputed; mu 0.5 and the 'braked' waist as before.
- **Next.** A per-pose leg preload (the largest the stance allows up to 0.1 N m) on the H1's two dropped poses and what it costs the residual; the tool-point spec as one table; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.
