## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 42 result: the G1's 1.3-point loss is all lean; the controller's four flipped sides move no stack by a digit but are needed to keep one re-leaned pose; and milestone 41's per-pose losses mixed two torque conventions; H161, H162 and H163 held, H164 refuted

`experiments/m42_sides/` (run, results, post hoc, figure; imports milestone 41's run, so both setups are the objects it built), `src/buildbot_sheathing/factorial.py` (new: the 2 × 2 split), `tests/test_factorial.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m42 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Which sides flipped.** Milestone 41's re-leaned pose set flipped four of the G1's six 'hold-min' sides: both hip rolls and both ankle rolls (the hip yaws kept theirs). The H1's four did not change.
- **The lean is all of it (H161, H162, H163 held).** At the G1's worst pose (1.2 m, 0) the 2 × 2 is 0.9160 / 0.9160 (milestone 32's lean, either sides) and 0.9030 / 0.9030 (zero-press lean, either sides): lean −1.30 points, sides 0.00, interaction 0.00. At every pose where both sides have a solution, on either lean and on both robots, the two sides give the same stack to the printed digit. The sides change which way a held roll joint is held, not whether it is held, and the stack counts only loose joints (post hoc: the held-joint count differs at one pose, G1 (1.2 m, +0.15 m), 21 vs 20, with the same stack).
- **But the flip is not free to undo (H164 refuted).** With milestone 38's sides, the re-leaned G1 at (1.2 m, +0.15 m) has no 'hold-min' solution; milestone 41's sides keep it (0.9363). So the side choice decides which poses the controller can hold, not how clean the sheets are where it can.
- **A correction to milestone 41 (post hoc, `posthoc.txt`).** Milestone 40's per-pose stack took the torques from the spec's plan (leg preload 0.1 N m and the squeeze); milestone 41's section C took milestone 38's 'hold-min' torques, and printed one against the other. The two conventions agree at each robot's worst pose (G1 0.9160, H1 0.9564), which is why milestone 41's headline stands, but differ at 9 of 34 poses by up to 0.97 points. In one convention the re-lean costs the G1 0.31–1.30 points at four poses and gains 0.60–0.79 at two, and costs the H1 **0.09–0.29** at five (milestone 41 printed 0.1–0.9 for the H1, and a 0.2-point gain at G1 (1.0 m, 0) that is a 0.32-point loss).
- **What it means.** The zero-press stance's cost in clean sheets is the stance itself; a designer cannot buy it back by keeping the controller's old sides, and should let the sides be re-chosen over the new pose set because that keeps one more pose holdable. The cost is up to 1.3 points on the G1 near the top of its reach and under 0.3 on the H1.
- **Not measured.** Statics; the stacks here use the 'hold-min' torques without milestone 37's leg preload and milestone 38's squeeze (milestone 41's convention), so the spec's own stack on the re-leaned poses is not yet computed; the 'braked' waist and mu 0.5 are as before.
- **Next.** The spec's stack (preload and squeeze plan) on the re-leaned poses, so the zero-press stance is costed in the convention the spec uses; the tool-point spec as one table; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 42 planned: is the G1's 1.3-point loss the lean or the controller's new sides?

- **Why.** Milestone 41 re-leaned 12 poses to stand at zero press and rebuilt milestone 38's setup on the new pose set. The G1's worst-pose no-hose stack ('braked') fell from 0.9160 to 0.9030, but two things changed at once: the six re-leaned G1 poses, and the 'hold-min' controller's chosen sides (`choose_signs` picks them by the worst LP margin over the whole pose set, so a new pose set can flip them). The H1's sides did not change and its re-leaned poses lost 0.1–0.9 points. A designer adopting the zero-press stance needs to know whether the cost is the stance (unavoidable) or the side choice (a controller setting that can be kept).
- **Model.** A 2 × 2 on the G1, no hose, 'braked' waist, everything else as milestone 41: leans {milestone 32's, re-leaned} × sides {milestone 38's, milestone 41's}. Two cells exist (0.9160 and 0.9030 at the worst pose); the two crossed cells are computed by re-running milestone 38's `torques` with the other set's sides (milestone 41's run is imported, so the poses, plans and stack code are the same objects). Per pose and at the worst pose: the lean's effect at fixed sides, the sides' effect at fixed lean, and the interaction. Which leg joints flipped is printed. The H1 is run the same way as a check (its sides did not change, so its crossed cells must equal its own).
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestone 41's published numbers are known; no crossed cell has been computed. If the old sides have no 'hold-min' solution at a re-leaned pose (or the new sides at an old pose), that pose drops from the cell and is printed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H161 (the lean is most of it).** With milestone 38's sides kept, the re-lean alone costs the G1's worst pose at least 0.65 points (half of the 1.30). Refuted if less.
  - **H162 (the sides alone are cheap).** On milestone 32's leans, swapping to milestone 41's sides moves the G1's worst-pose stack by at most 0.5 points. Refuted if more.
  - **H163 (no large interaction).** At the G1's worst pose, |both − lean only − sides only| is at most 0.3 points. Refuted if more.
  - **H164 (the old sides still hold).** Milestone 38's G1 sides keep a 'hold-min' solution at all six re-leaned G1 poses. Refuted if any loses it.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 41 result: every leaned stance can be re-leaned to stand at zero press and still reach 50 N, and the 0.01–0.02 mm spec holds; but the G1 at 1.2 m keeps only 1.6 N above the window, one H1 pose loses its controller, and the G1's worst stack drops 1.3 points; H156 and H159 held, H157, H158 and H160 refuted

`src/buildbot_sheathing/stance0.py` (new: the bisection on the lean, the window test, the rigid-body window width), `experiments/m41_stance0/` (run, results, figure, post hoc ceiling; runs milestone 38's import twice, with milestone 32's leans and with the re-leans, so every plan, side choice and calibration is recomputed), `tests/test_stance0.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m41 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **A zero-press lean exists everywhere (H156 held).** All 12 poses that needed a press (milestone 40) have a lean that balances over the whole of 0–50 N: the G1 at z 1.0 m tops out at 58.6–62.2 N, at 1.2 m at **51.6–51.7 N**; the H1 at 1.4 m at 67.2 N, at 1.6 m at 58.7 N.
- **The re-lean is not small (H157 refuted).** The balanced centre of mass moves back 9.9–39.1 mm: 18 mm (G1, 1.0 m) and 39 mm (G1, 1.2 m), 10 and 24 mm (H1, 1.4 and 1.6 m).
- **Check, and why the window cannot be wider.** At every re-leaned pose the simulated window's width equals (m + m_tool) g × sole length / grip height to the printed digit (G1 33.34 kg, H1 51.44 kg, sole 0.170 and 0.175 m, tool 3.4 kg): the centre of pressure crosses the whole sole between 0 N and the top. So no lean gives more. Post hoc, from that formula: a stance that stands at zero press and still balances at 50 N exists only below a grip height of **1.225 m (G1)** and **1.883 m (H1)**; the G1's top poses sit 4 cm under its ceiling, which is why they keep only 1.6 N of margin above a 50 N press. At lower poses friction or the joint geometry binds first (the analytic width overstates the simulated one).
- **One pose loses its controller (H158 refuted).** 11 of 12 re-leaned poses keep a 'hold-min' solution at both window ends; the H1 at (1.6 m, −0.15 m) does not and drops out, as the H1 at (0.4 m, −0.15 m) already had. The G1 controller's chosen sides changed with the new pose set (they are chosen over the set); the H1's did not.
- **The tool-point spec holds (H159 held).** Braked waist, |e| 0.2 N, S 10 N, m 0.1 N m: the G1's worst residual along the wall is 0.016 / **0.011** / 0.011 mm at 0 / 5 / 20 N of drag (milestone 40: 0.021 / 0.012 / 0.012), the H1's 0.010 / **0.019** / 0.032 (unchanged). Over the re-leaned poses alone the worst is 0.016 mm (G1) and 0.014 mm (H1).
- **The stack pays (H160 refuted).** No hose, braked: the G1's worst pose, (1.2 m, 0), falls from 0.9160 to **0.9030** (1.30 points); among the re-leaned poses three G1 poses lose 0.3–1.3 points and two gain 0.2–0.6, and every H1 pose loses 0.1–0.9. The H1's worst pose over the set rises 0.46 points only because its old worst pose, (1.6 m, −0.15 m), lost its controller and left the set: that is not a gain. Which part of the G1's loss is the lean and which the changed sides is not separated here.
- **What it means.** "A stance that balances at zero press" can join the spec at every pose tried, at a cost of up to 1.3 points of clean sheets and, for the G1 near the top of a wall, almost no margin above the press (1.6 N); the ceiling is a formula a designer can check before buying: grip height < (m + m_tool) g × sole length / F_max.
- **Not measured.** Statics only (the lift-off and the lean change between nails are not simulated); the 'hold-min' controller and mu 0.5 are milestone 32's; the stack's split between lean and side choice is not separated; the sole length is the Menagerie collision geometry, not a measured foot.
- **Next.** The paper's tool-point spec as one table (sensor, leg preload, squeeze, waist brake, zero-press stance and its height ceiling); separate the lean from the side choice in the G1's 1.3 points; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 41 planned: a stance that stands at zero press, and what it costs in the window

- **Why.** Milestone 40 found that 12 of milestone 32's 35 leaned poses balance only above a press of 3.7–11.8 N (the G1's six at z 1.0 and 1.2 m, milestone 30's calibration pose among them; the H1's six at 1.4 and 1.6 m). Milestone 32 chose each lean so the centre of pressure sits at the middle of the sole under the mid-window press (37.5 N); a nailer whose recoil lifts the nose then leaves those robots leaning on nothing. The fix an engineer would try first is to lean less. Both edges of a stance's balancing window rise with its lean, so the lean that stands at zero press and keeps the most of the window above it is the furthest-forward one that still stands at 0 N.
- **Model.** For each pose whose window starts above 0 N, the balanced centre of mass ahead of the feet centre (`com_dx`) is bisected between 0 and its milestone 32 lean for the largest value at which the stance balances at zero press (same IK, same contact cones, mu 0.5; tolerance 0.5 mm); every other pose keeps its lean. A re-leaned pose whose window covers 0–50 N replaces the original, and milestone 38's whole setup is rebuilt on the new pose set (its import runs again with the lean swapped, so the controller's chosen sides, plans, squeeze, calibration at milestone 30's pose and torques are recomputed, not patched). Then, with milestone 39's 'braked' waist (milestone 40's spec), S* 10 N, m 0.1 N m, rubber hose, k_t 1e4: (B) the worst residual along the wall over all poses and over the re-leaned ones, |e| 0.2 N, H 0, 5 and 20 N, against milestone 40's 'uniform' column; (C) the no-hose clean-sheet stack per pose against milestone 40's. An analytic check: a rigid stance's window is (m + m_tool) g × sole length / grip height wide.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestones 32–40's published numbers and code have been read; no pose has been re-leaned and no window, residual or stack at a new lean computed. If milestone 30's calibration pose has no zero-press lean it keeps its lean, for calibration only, and that is printed. The re-lean is quasi-static like everything since milestone 30; nothing here simulates the lift-off itself.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H156 (a zero-press lean exists).** Every one of the 12 poses has a lean that balances over the whole of 0–50 N. Refuted if any does not.
  - **H157 (the re-lean is small).** At every re-leaned pose the balanced centre of mass moves back by at most 20 mm. Refuted if more anywhere.
  - **H158 (the controller still holds).** Every re-leaned pose that covers 0–50 N keeps a 'hold-min' solution at both window ends. Refuted if any loses it.
  - **H159 (the spec survives).** 'braked', |e| 0.2 N, a 5 N drag: the worst residual along the wall over all poses is at most 0.1 mm on both robots (milestone 40: G1 0.012, H1 0.019). Refuted if either exceeds it.
  - **H160 (the stack survives).** 'braked', no hose: each robot's worst-pose stack loses at most 0.5 points against milestone 40's (G1 0.9160, H1 0.9564). Refuted if either loses more.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 40 result: the nailer's recoil (0.09–8.7 N s, from published measurements) lifts the nose, and the re-press from zero sets none of the loose joints, so milestone 39's spec stands; but a third of the leaned stances cannot stand without the press; H153 held, H151, H152, H154 and H155 refuted

`src/buildbot_sheathing/liftoff.py` (new: the recoil bracket, the set test on a rising press, the post hoc entry test), `experiments/m40_liftoff/` (run, results, post hoc census, figure; imports milestone 39's run, so every pose, plan and residual is the same code), `tests/test_liftoff.py` (5 tests), `reproduce.py` (m40 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph, two references), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **The recoil, bracketed.** An 8d common shank is 4.33 g; at the chronograph's 29.8–36.9 m/s (Buchalter et al. 2002) it carries 0.129–0.160 N s, at Peterson and Dixon's 20–30 m/s 0.087–0.130 N s. That is a floor (the piston and driver add to it). Lowe et al. (2014)'s force-plate unloading, taken at face value, is 8.7 N s per transfer phase, an upper figure for the user and tool together. The bracket 0.087–8.7 N s straddles the 0.25–1 N s band where [`buildbot-recoil`](../buildbot-recoil)'s nose leaves the wall.
- **Check.** With the 'uniform' compensator every residual equals milestone 39's to 0.0 mm (G1 braked 5 N 0.012, stock 0.485; H1 braked 0.019).
- **One post hoc change, disclosed.** The smoke run found that a leaned pose balances only above a press: the G1's six poses at z 1.0 and 1.2 m (milestone 30's calibration pose among them) need 6.4 and 11.6–11.8 N, the H1's six at 1.4 and 1.6 m need 3.7 and 8.0 N. Below that the stance tips towards the wall. So the rise starts where the stance first balances under the load at hand (samples that balance under no controller were skipped: 15,024 of 97,762 in section B), not at F_r. **That is itself the finding with consequences:** at 12 of 35 poses, a recoil that lifts the nose leaves the robot leaning on nothing, so the stance must be planned to balance at zero press (or the controller must catch the lean) before a sequential-trip shot.
- **The re-press sets nothing at the census (H151 refuted, H155 refuted).** At H 0 with an exact sensor, 56 nose-moving joints are loose in the window (G1 41, H1 15). Only 11 (all G1: 10 arm, 1 waist) sit below tau_f over the whole window, and none of them reaches tau_f on the rise: the largest |tau| any of them reaches is 0.159 N m (F_r 0) against tau_f 0.2. F_r 10 N sets none either (0 vs 0, so 'fewer' is refuted).
- **Why the uniform model survives (post hoc census, `posthoc.txt`).** Of the 56, 36 change sign inside the 25–50 N window and 7 rise through tau_f inside it: where they sit depends on the force at which the tool fires, not on how the nose got there. Only 2 enter the window above tau_f with one sign, which a rising press would set; this test was not registered.
- **Milestone 39's compensator keeps the spec (H152 refuted, H153 held).** Braked waist, |e| 0.2 N, 5 N drag: the G1's worst residual is 0.012 mm with lift-off and either compensator. In one registered cell it does matter: at a 20 N drag, at pose (0.6 m, −0.15 m) with the hose pulling along −y, the rise sets the G1's right wrist yaw (−0.18 to +0.08 N m in the window, −0.44 to −0.23 on the rise), and the unaware compensator is left **0.203 mm** where the aware one keeps 0.012 (stock and braked alike).
- **The stack does not move (H154 refuted).** With no joint set at H 0, the no-hose stack at the G1's worst pose is 0.9160 either way (braked; 0.9081 stock), the H1's 0.9564.
- **What it means.** The recoil does lift the nose, but the tool-point spec of milestones 36–39 (current F/T sensor, leg preload, 10 N squeeze, braked waist) does not depend on how the nose returns: the joints it leaves loose are loose because the firing force moves them, and only a heavy drag lets the rise set one (a compensator that plans the rise removes that 0.2 mm). What the recoil does change is the stance: a leaned stance that needs the press to stand must not be used with a tool that lifts off.
- **Not measured.** The rise is quasi-static (no recoil transient is simulated, and a joint the transient sets and the rise does not reach stays loose); the recoil enters only as whether the nose lifts; tau_f 0.2 N m is milestone 37's unsourced figure (a tau_f below 0.16 N m would let the rise set some joints); balance below the window is not modelled beyond saying it fails.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec as one table (sensor, leg preload, squeeze, waist brake, a stance that balances at zero press); a stance chosen to balance over 0–50 N and what it costs in the window; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 40 planned: the recoil's lift-off and the re-press from zero

- **Why.** Every milestone since 30 scores a joint whose torque stays below the holding threshold tau_f over the 25–50 N press window as *loose*: anywhere in its gap, uniformly, at every nail. That assumes nothing about how the nose got onto the wall. But the nose does not stay there between nails. The recoil lifts it: [`buildbot-recoil`](../buildbot-recoil) found 0.5 N s loses the wall in most arm loops and 1 N s in all of them, and its recoil was swept because no figure was found. A sequential-trip tool must also release its contact arm before the next shot, and the robot moves 6 or 12 in to the next nail. So each nail starts from a lifted nose and a press that rises from (near) zero. A loose joint whose torque passes tau_f somewhere on that rise is left on that side when the window is reached: a repeatable bias, not a uniform scatter. Milestone 39's compensator does not model it.
- **Sources for the recoil, read before registering.** (1) Nail velocity from pneumatic nailers, ballistic chronograph: 97.7–121.1 ft/s, mean 105.5 (29.8–36.9 m/s; Buchalter et al. 2002, *Ear Nose Throat J* 81:779–783, as quoted in NIOSH's 2013 Science Blog post on nail-gun speed); Peterson and Dixon (1975) 20–30 m/s at 2 m (same post). An 8d common shank (0.131 in by 2.5 in, steel 7850 kg/m³) is 4.33 g, so the nail alone carries 0.087–0.160 N s: a floor, since the piston and driver's momentum also goes into the tool. (2) Lowe, Albers and Hudock (2014, *Int J Ind Ergon* 44(5):715–722, doi:10.1016/j.ergon.2014.06.007, Appendix B): force-plate ground reaction under two users nailing flat, cumulative load 32.2 N against the tool-and-hose weight's 42.2 N over the 0.87 s transfer phase, read by the authors as recoil unloading 24% of the weight. Taken at face value that is (42.2 − 32.2) × 0.87 = 8.7 N s per nail, an upper figure for the whole user-tool system (the paper's own wording of the baseline is inconsistent, and the user's arm motion is in it). The bracket 0.09–8.7 N s straddles the 0.25–1 N s band where `buildbot-recoil`'s nose leaves the wall, so lift-off cannot be ruled out, and the release and the move between nails lift it anyway.
- **Model.** Milestone 39's spec (S* = 10 N squeeze, m = 0.1 N m leg preload, settle model, rubber hose, k_t 1e4, one calibration at milestone 30's pose in the same hose state and sensor error), cases 'stock' and 'braked' (waist). New: the press is quasi-static from a release force F_r up to the window, sampled at F_r, F_r + 5, ..., 20 N with the same controller (hold QP and leg preload at each F, falling back to the hold QP alone where the preload has no solution, counted) and the window's squeeze. A joint below tau_f at both window ends is *set* if its torque reaches tau_f at some sampled F on the rise: it then sits at the side of the last such sample, a bias of −sign(tau) gap / 2. Otherwise it stays loose. The robot's set sides come from its settled torques, the compensator's from its plan. (A) Census at H 0 with an exact sensor: nose-moving joints loose in the window, and how many the re-press sets, by kind, for F_r 0 and 10 N. (B) Worst residual along the wall over poses, eight hose directions and six signed sensor axes, |e| 0.2 N, H 0, 5 and 20 N, F_r 0: with milestone 39's compensator ('unaware', which takes loose joints as zero mean) and one that predicts the set sides from its own plan ('aware'). (D) The no-hose clean-sheet stack per pose with the spec's window torques: loose joints uniform (as milestones 30–39) against set joints carrying no random part.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestones 37–39's published numbers and code and the sources above have been read; no torque below 25 N of press has been computed. The press is quasi-static (no recoil transient is simulated; a joint the transient leaves set and the re-press does not reach stays loose, which is what it was). The recoil figures are the bracket above, used only to say whether lift-off happens.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H151 (the re-press sets most loose joints).** F_r 0, H 0, exact sensor: over both robots' poses, at least half the nose-moving joints loose in the window are set by the re-press. Refuted if fewer.
  - **H152 (milestone 39's compensator loses the spec).** 'braked', F_r 0, |e| 0.2 N, a 5 N drag: the G1's worst residual along the wall with the unaware compensator exceeds 0.1 mm (milestone 39: 0.012, with uniform loose joints). Refuted if 0.1 mm or less.
  - **H153 (an aware compensator keeps it).** Same cell with the aware compensator: at most 0.1 mm. Refuted if more.
  - **H154 (the stack gains).** 'braked': the no-hose stack at the G1's worst pose rises by at least 0.5 points when set joints carry no random part. Refuted if less.
  - **H155 (a partial release sets fewer).** H151's census with F_r 10 N counts fewer set joints than with F_r 0. Refuted if as many or more.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 39 result: braking the waist takes the floor from 0.49 to 0.01 mm and a 1 arcmin waist to 0.11; locking roll and pitch, as on the base G1, changes nothing because the yaw is what flips; H146–H150 held

`src/buildbot_sheathing/waist.py` (new: the per-joint gap of each waist case), `experiments/m39_waist/` (run, results, figure; imports milestone 38's run so every pose, plan and residual is the same code), `tests/test_waist.py` (3 tests), `reproduce.py` (m39 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Check.** 'stock' reproduces milestone 38's S = 10 N residuals exactly (largest difference 0.0 mm) and its stacks to the printed digit (G1 0–10 N 5.80, 0–20 N 0.05; H1 0.02 and 0.02).
- **Braked (H146 held).** With the waist joints rigid and the FT 300 (|e| 0.2 N), the G1's worst residual along the wall at a 5 N drag falls from 0.485 to **0.012 mm**, at 0 N from 0.479 to 0.021. The H1 at 5 N 0.501 → 0.019, at 20 N 0.423 → 0.032.
- **The base G1's lock does not help (H147 held, at full strength).** Making waist roll and pitch rigid (the G1 ships with only the yaw; unitree.com/g1) leaves 0.485 mm at 5 N and 5.80 points over 0–10 N: the waist joint that flips is the yaw, the one every G1 keeps.
- **A precision waist gearbox nearly does it (H148 held).** 1 arcmin on the waist leaves the G1 0.106 mm at 5 N (0.099 at 0 N) and the H1 0.098 and 0.090. The floor scales with the waist gap, as the held-bias model says (gap / 2 on a flipped joint).
- **The stacks close (H149 held).** G1 0–10 N: stock 5.80, yaw only 5.80, precision 0.11, **braked 0.04 points**; 0–20 N 0.05 → 0.03. The H1 stays at 0.02 in every case (its stacks were already set by other joints). The no-hose stack the loss is counted from rises slightly at the worst G1 pose (0.9081 → 0.9160) because a rigid waist adds no random error when loose.
- **The old sensor (H150 held).** With |e| 2.4 N a braked waist still leaves 0.873 mm at 5 N (G1) and 0.818 mm at 0 N; the 2.4 N error flips arm and leg joints by more than any preload here.
- **What it means.** With a current wrist F/T sensor, the leg preload (milestone 37) and a 10 N squeeze (milestone 38), the last 0.5 mm is the waist yaw's backlash. A spec can close it with a waist yaw holding brake (or lock) while nailing, or a waist gearbox at about 1 arcmin, which gives about 0.1 mm. Dropping the optional roll and pitch joints, as the base G1 does, buys nothing.
- **Not measured.** The brake is ideal (no gap, no compliance); the lock holds at the posed angle (waist pitch up to ±29.8°, roll ±8°, yaw ±5° over the G1's poses), whereas the base G1's roll and pitch are fixed at zero, so its poses would differ; poses are not re-solved. No waist backlash figure is published for either robot; 1 and 5 arcmin are the low end and middle of the catalogue range already cited. Statics only.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec (sensor, leg preload, squeeze, waist brake) as one table; the nailer's recoil; the G1's over-limit wrist pitch at low poses.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 39 planned: the waist's gap, from stock to braked

- **Why.** Milestone 38's squeeze cleared every arm flip and left a floor of 0.48–0.50 mm (G1 at 0 and 5 N of hose drag, H1 at 5 N) that sits in the waist (G1) and torso (H1) joints and two leg joints. No ground reaction and no squeeze moves a waist torque: the tool's wrench alone sets it. What is left to an engineer is the waist's own hardware: its gearbox backlash, or a lock. Unitree sells the G1 with one waist joint (yaw) and the roll and pitch joints as an option ("Waist Degrees of Freedom 1, 1+(Optional 2 additional waist degrees of freedom)", unitree.com/g1, read 2026-11-17); Menagerie's G1 model has all three.
- **Model.** Milestone 38's setup at its S* = 10 N squeeze and m = 0.1 N m leg preload (settle model, rubber hose, k_t 1e4, one calibration at milestone 30's pose in the same hose state and sensor error). New: the total gap is set per joint. Waist cases: 'stock' (5 arcmin on every joint, milestone 38), 'yaw only' (G1: waist roll and pitch rigid at the posed angle, as on the one-waist-joint G1; yaw 5 arcmin), 'precision' (1 arcmin on the waist joints, the low end of the 1–7 arcmin catalogue precision-planetary range the paper already cites) and 'braked' (0 on the waist joints: a holding brake or a lock at the posed angle). Every other joint keeps 5 arcmin. Torques do not depend on the gap, so milestone 38's flip census stands; what changes is what a flip costs. (B) the worst residual along the wall and vertically over poses, eight hose directions and six signed sensor axes, |e| 0.2 and 2.4 N, H 0, 5 and 20 N, per case; (D) the 0–10 and 0–20 N stacks with the FT 300 per case, and the no-hose stack the loss is counted from.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestone 38's published numbers, the code and the waist joint names and ranges in the two MJCFs have been read; no residual or stack with a per-joint gap has been computed. Locking at the posed angle is not the same as the one-waist-joint G1, whose roll and pitch are fixed at zero; the poses are not re-solved.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H146 (a braked waist clears the G1's floor).** 'braked', |e| 0.2 N, a 5 N drag: the G1's worst residual along the wall is at most 0.1 mm (milestone 38: 0.485). Refuted if more.
  - **H147 (yaw alone keeps most of it).** 'yaw only', same cell: the G1's worst residual is at least half the 'stock' figure. Refuted if less.
  - **H148 (a precision waist gearbox is nearly as good).** 'precision', same cell: at most 0.15 mm. Refuted if more.
  - **H149 (the stacks close).** 'braked': the G1's 0–10 N stack loses at most 1 point (milestone 38: 5.80) and every other robot and range at most 0.1. Refuted if either fails.
  - **H150 (the old sensor stays unusable).** 'braked', |e| 2.4 N, a 5 N drag: the G1 still leaves more than 0.5 mm. Refuted if 0.5 mm or less.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 38 result: a two-hand squeeze clears every arm flip and takes 0–20 N of hose drag from 1.6 points lost to 0.05; the waist joints set the floor that is left (0.48–0.50 mm); H141, H143, H144 and H145 held, H142 refuted

`src/buildbot_sheathing/squeeze.py` (new: the squeeze's direction and generalised force, the controller's grid choice, the MJCF torque limits), `experiments/m38_squeeze/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_squeeze.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m38 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Checks.** With S = 0 every residual and stack equals milestone 37's m = 0.1 row to the printed digit (G1 5 N 0.830 mm, 0–20 N 1.64 points; H1 2.19). Per newton of squeeze, the base's generalised force is at most 2.2e-16 and every leg and waist torque 1.1e-16 N m, while an arm joint moves by up to 0.47 N m (H141 held): the squeeze is a pure arm preload, as the statics say.
- **One post hoc change, disclosed.** The registered choice kept every arm joint inside its MJCF torque limit. The smoke run (before the full run) found that the G1's right wrist pitch already carries more than its 5 N m rating with no squeeze at 7 of 14 poses (the low ones, z 0.4–0.8 m; up to 1.21 times the limit), so the registered rule had no solution there. The run instead forbids the squeeze from pushing any arm joint beyond the larger of its limit and its unsqueezed torque. That over-limit wrist is itself a finding: holding the 3.4 kg nailer with its nose 0.3 m ahead of the grip exceeds the G1's listed wrist-pitch torque at low poses.
- **The arm flips go (H143 held).** At |e| 0.2 N (FT 300) over both robots and H 0, 5 and 20 N, arm flips fall from 14 to 0 at S = 10, 20 and 40 N. The waist count rises from 7 to 9 and the other leg from 1 to 2. The squeeze cannot move those joints' torques; the census takes each pose's worst hose direction and sensor axis, and those change once the arm flips are gone.
- **Where it helps and where it does not (H144 held, barely).** G1, |e| 0.2 N: 5 N drag 0.830 → 0.485 mm, 20 N 0.285 → 0.012; 0 N stays 0.479. H1: 0 N 0.373 → 0.010; 5 N stays 0.501, 20 N 0.423. Every cell that stays put is a waist (G1 waist, H1 torso) or leg flip. The floor left is 0.48–0.50 mm at worst.
- **Stacks.** 0–20 N with the FT 300's noise: G1 1.64 → 0.05 points, H1 2.19 → 0.02. 0–10 N: H1 6.92 → 0.02; G1 6.56 → 5.80 (the worst pose moves to z 0.4 m, dy 0.15 m, where the waist sets it). S* = 10 N, the smallest bound that reaches section B's best.
- **The margin it buys (H142 refuted).** With an exact sensor at H 0 the smallest arm margin over the G1's poses is 0.040 N m at S 10 N, 0.070 at 20 N and 0.129 at 40 N (13 of 14 poses reach 0.1 N m at 20 N). The H1's four moving arm joints reach 0.71 N m at 10 N. The controller takes the full bound every time (it maximises the margin), at up to 0.99 of the H1's arm limits at S 40.
- **The old sensor gets worse in some cells.** With |e| 2.4 N the squeeze raises the worst residual at H 0 (G1 0.566 → 0.818 mm, H1 0.504 → 0.928) and leaves 1.09–1.68 mm at 20 N. A 2.4 N error moves arm torques by more than the margins here, and the squeeze changes which joints sit on which side. Not diagnosed further; the rule stands that a pre-2017-class sensor is not usable.
- **What it means.** With a current wrist F/T sensor, the robot should hold its leg joints 0.1 N m clear of the threshold through the ground reaction (milestone 37) and its arm joints clear through a two-hand squeeze of 10 N or more along the grip line. Together they take 0–20 N of hose drag to 0.05 points. What remains is the waist (and the H1's torso), whose torque only the tool's wrench sets. Clearing it would mean a different grip (moving where the hands take the wrench) or less backlash in the waist.
- **Not measured.** No hand grip force is published for either robot, so S is swept. The squeeze is modelled as an ideal force pair at the grip points (no slip, no tool compliance). The settle model and tau_f (0.2 N m, unsourced) are milestone 37's. Statics only.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec (the stack of remedies: sensor, leg preload, squeeze); a grip that changes the waist's share; the nailer's recoil.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 38 planned: a two-hand squeeze as the arm-joint preload

- **Why.** Milestone 37's leg preload (m = 0.1 N m) took the hold set out of the backlash lottery, but left 0.3–0.8 mm from 7 waist and 14 arm joints that sit near the 0.2 N m holding threshold tau_f, which no choice of ground reaction can move. Its 'Next' named an internal squeeze: the robot holds the tool in two hands, so it can push the hands towards (or away from) each other along the line between them. That force pair has zero net force and zero net moment, so it leaves the tool's wrench, the ground reaction, the leg torques and the waist torques unchanged and moves only the arm joints. It is the arms' counterpart of milestone 37's preload, and it cannot reach the waist.
- **Model.** Milestone 37's setup and settle model (G1 and H1 leaned poses, 'hold-min' sides, leg preload m = 0.1 N m, rubber hose, k_t 1e4, one calibration at milestone 30's pose in the same hose state and sensor error). New: a squeeze s (N) along the unit vector between the two grip points, +s on the left hand and −s on the right, held constant through the press. The controller picks s from its plan (measured force) by a grid over [−S, S] (0.05 N steps) that maximises the smallest distance ||tau_j| − tau_f| over the arm joints that move the nose, at both press ends, subject to every arm joint staying inside its actuator's torque limit in the Menagerie model; the same s acts on the robot when it settles under the true force. S in {0, 10, 20, 40} N (no grip-force figure is published for either robot's hand, so S is swept, not sourced). (A) the flip census (hold set, other leg, waist, arm) at |e| 0.2 N; (B) the worst residual along the wall and vertically over poses, eight hose directions and six signed sensor axes, |e| 0.2 and 2.4 N, H 0, 5 and 20 N, per S; (B2) the arm margin the squeeze achieves and the |s| it uses at H 0 with an exact sensor; (D) the 0–10 and 0–20 N stacks with the FT 300 at the S that section B picks.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestone 37's published numbers and the existing code have been read; no squeeze, census or residual with a squeeze has been computed.

  - **H142 (a squeeze clears the arms).** With S = 20 N, an exact sensor and H 0, the smallest arm margin is at least 0.1 N m at every G1 pose with a plan. Refuted if any pose falls short.

  - **H144 (the floor drops).** With S = 20 N, m = 0.1 N m, |e| 0.2 N and a 5 N drag, the G1's worst residual along the wall is at most 0.5 mm (milestone 37: 0.830). Refuted if more.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 37 planned: a held-joint preload against the sensor's floor

- **Why.** Milestone 36 left a floor of 0.3–0.5 mm with a good wrist F/T sensor: any measurement error flips a joint whose torque sits 0.001–0.05 N m from the 0.2 N m holding threshold tau_f, and the compensator then subtracts (or leaves) half a gap. Its 'Next' named a preload: a balance controller can move torque between the legs through the internal ground-reaction forces (milestone 32), so it can command each leg joint's torque at least a margin m away from tau_f, on whichever side it already is. The waist and arm torques are set by the tool's wrench alone (no choice of ground reaction changes them, `grf_qp` docstring), so a preload cannot reach a flip there.
- **A more honest error model.** Milestone 36 took the robot's torques as the controller's plan under the true force and the compensator's as the plan under the measured force, as if the controller knew the truth. Here the controller (and the compensator, which shares its model) plans once from the measured force f + e; the robot then settles under the true force f, modelled as the smallest change of the planned contact forces that restores the base's balance (min-norm, `pinv(Aeq)`), and its joint torques follow. This 'settle' model is a stand-in for the joint-level feedback, not a measurement; milestone 36's 're-solve' convention is reported alongside at m = 0.
- **Model.** Milestone 36's setup (G1 and H1 leaned poses, 'hold-min' sides, rubber hose, k_t 1e4, one calibration at milestone 30's pose in the same hose state and sensor error). Controller 'hold-margin m': milestone 32's 'hold-min' QP, then for every leg joint and each press end, the end's torque pushed to at least tau_f + m if it is at or above tau_f, else to at most tau_f − m in magnitude; m in {0, 0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3} N m. (A) which joints flip at m = 0 (hold set, other leg, waist, arm); (B) the worst residual along the wall and vertically over poses, eight hose directions and six signed sensor axes, for the FT 300 (|e| 0.2 N) and the pre-2017 FT 300 (2.4 N), at H 0, 5 and 20 N, per m; what it costs (poses that lose a solution, the rise in the largest leg torque); (C) a hedged compensator that subtracts a quarter gap, not half, on joints whose planned |tau| is within a band g of tau_f (g 0.02–0.1 N m), with and without the preload; (D) the 0–20 N stack with the FT 300 at the m that section B picks.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestone 36's published numbers and the `grf_qp` code have been read; no residual, flip classification or preloaded solution has been computed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H136 (the flips are in the legs).** Under the settle model at m = 0, with |e| 0.2 N, over both robots and H 0, 5 and 20 N, at least half of the mispredicted joint-pose cases (wrong, spurious or missed, summed over the worst sensor axis and direction per pose) are leg joints. Refuted if fewer than half.
  - **H137 (a preload removes the floor).** With the FT 300 (|e| 0.2 N) and m = 0.1 N m, a 5 N drag leaves the G1's worst pose at most 0.1 mm along the wall. Refuted if more.
  - **H138 (the preload is cheap).** m = 0.1 N m loses no G1 pose that m = 0 solves at H 0 and raises the largest leg-joint |tau| at any G1 pose by at most 10%. Refuted if either fails.
  - **H139 (the old sensor needs more).** With |e| 2.4 N, m = 0.1 N m still leaves more than 0.3 mm at a 5 N drag (G1). Refuted if 0.3 mm or less.
  - **H140 (hedging halves it).** Without a preload, the hedged compensator (g 0.05 N m) leaves the G1 at a 5 N drag with |e| 0.2 N at most half of the plain compensator's worst |y|. Refuted if more than half.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 36 result: a wrist F/T sensor removes the hose's windup but not its backlash lottery; any sensor error flips a held joint sitting at the friction threshold; H132 and H134 refuted, H133 and H135 held

`src/buildbot_sheathing/wrist_ft.py` (new: the joint error with a measured load and a misjudged stiffness, the listed sensors, span arithmetic), `experiments/m36_wrist_ft/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_wrist_ft.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m36 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Checks.** The no-sensor case reproduces milestone 35's stacks to the printed digit (G1 2.17, 20.66, 91.55; H1 4.39, 34.28, 67.81 points). An exact sensor with k_t known leaves 0 everywhere.
- **The sensor removes most of it (A).** Worst residual along the wall at the worst G1 pose, no sensor against a 0.2 N error (2 sigma of the current Robotiq FT 300): H 0 0.49 → 0.48 mm, 5 N 0.97 → 0.82, 10 N 2.09 → 0.48, 20 N 2.34 → 0.29. The pre-2017 FT 300 (2.4 N) leaves 0.75–1.33 mm (H133 held). H1: 0.37–0.50 mm with the FT 300, 0.51–0.86 mm with the old one.
- **But not below about 0.3–0.5 mm, whatever the sensor (H132 refuted: 0.824 mm at 5 N).** At H 0 a 0.125 N error leaves 0.478 mm, a 1 N error 0.497 mm. The post hoc section A2 (added after section A's first rows printed, not registered) shows why: at 5 N a 0.001 N error leaves 0.000 mm, 0.01 N already 0.267 mm (one joint predicted held that is loose), 0.2 N 0.824 mm (one spurious, one missed). The flipped joints sit 0.0014–0.05 N m from the 0.2 N m holding threshold tau_f. A sharp threshold turns any measurement error into a half-gap error on a joint at its edge; that is a property of milestone 30's held/loose model (tau_f unsourced, swept 0.05–0.5 there), and a real joint's transition is probably gradual, so this floor is an upper bound on the model's brittleness, not a measurement.
- **The stiffness matters less than registered (H134 refuted).** With an exact sensor and k_t misjudged by +25% or −25%, a 5 N drag leaves 0.060 and 0.100 mm along the wall (0.17 and 0.28 mm vertically); 20 N 0.24 and 0.39 mm. One calibration takes out most of the misjudged windup because it is a near-common bias at 5 N.
- **Stacks (C).** With the drag uniform over 0–20 N, the FT 300 class costs the worst G1 pose 1.64 points (H135 held; 91.55 without the sensor), the Mini45 class 0.02, the pre-2017 FT 300 33.6. Over 0–10 N both current sensors cost 6.57 points (20.66 without); the pre-2017 one 87 (worse than no sensor: 2.4 N of bias costs more than a hose it does not know). The exact sensor with k_t ±25% costs at most 1.22 points (G1, 0–20 N, −25%). The losses are not monotone in H_max, because which joints sit at the threshold changes with the hose state.
- **What it means.** A wrist F/T sensor is the right remedy for the windup and the controller's balance (with k_t known to 25% it leaves at most 0.4 mm at 20 N), but it does not make model-based backlash compensation robust: a compensator that subtracts half a gap from every joint it predicts held is a lottery on the few joints near the threshold, and those exist at most poses. A spec should either preload those joints away from the threshold (a commanded bias torque) or drop backlash compensation and buy joints with less backlash. A pre-2017-class sensor (1.2 N noise) is worse than none.
- **Not measured.** Sensor bias drift is swept, not sourced; the Mini45's figure is its resolution, used as a noise proxy (no accuracy is published); moment errors are not modelled; noise-driven flips per shot are not in the stack (the noise enters linearised); tau_f is milestone 30's 0.2 N m; statics only. The H1's 0.4 m, −0.15 m pose has no 'hold-min' solution and is skipped.
- **Next.** A held-joint preload (a commanded bias torque that keeps each held joint at least a margin above tau_f) and the margin it needs against a given sensor; the paper's headline for a tool-point spec; the nailer's recoil.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 36 planned: a wrist force-torque sensor as the hose's remedy

- **Why.** Milestone 35 left the matched compensator 1.0 mm along the wall at a 5 N hose drag, and named three remedies: take the hose off the tool, keep the drag small, or measure the tool's wrench. The third is the one a controls engineer reaches for. A wrist F/T sensor turns the unknown hose force into a measured one: the compensator predicts each held joint's side and the transmission windup from the measured wrench instead of the no-hose model. What is left is the sensor's error and any error in the joints' stiffness k_t, which no datasheet gives (milestone 34).
- **Sources.** Robotiq FT 300 (instruction manual, specifications table): signal noise 0.1 N on Fx, Fy, Fz (standard deviation over 1 s), 0.005 N m on Mx, My; the version sold before 10/2017 listed 1.2 N (Fx, Fy) and 0.5 N (Fz). ATI Mini45, SI-145-5 calibration (ati-ia.com product page, read 2026-11-16): resolution 1/16 N on Fx, Fy and Fz after dropping four counts of noise; no accuracy figure is published ("contact your sales representative"). A sensor's bias drift between tares is not published by either, so the error magnitude is also swept (0.125-2.4 N).
- **Model.** On milestone 35's setup (G1 and H1 leaned poses on 'hold-min', rubber hose, k_t 1e4), the robot's actual torques are the QP solution under the true hose force f; the compensator's are the QP solution under the measured force f + e. The joint-space error is the backlash misprediction delta(t) - delta(t_hat) plus the windup error (the true windup at k_t minus the predicted one at k_t (1 + eps)), calibrated once at milestone 30's pose in the same hose state with the same sensor error. (A) the residual along the wall and vertically, worst pose, worst of the six signed axis directions of e (|e| = 2 sigma: ATI 0.125 N, FT 300 0.2 N, pre-2017 FT 300 2.4 N; also 0.5 and 1 N) at H 0, 5 (eight directions phi), 10 and 20 N (milestone 35's worst phi); (B) k_t misjudged by +-25% with an exact sensor; (C) the stack with H uniform over [0, H_max]: the sensor's noise per shot as a scatter (linearised through the QP per axis), the k_t error's share of the windup spread as a scatter, and the residual at H_max / 2 as a bias, against milestone 35's unmodelled stack. Noise-driven backlash flips per shot are not modelled.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only the two datasheets above and milestones 33-35's published numbers have been read; no residual, stack or sensor case has been computed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H132 (a sensor fixes the hose).** With a 0.2 N error (2 sigma of the current FT 300) and k_t known, a 5 N drag in the worst direction leaves the matched compensator at most 0.1 mm along the wall at the worst G1 pose. Refuted if more.
  - **H133 (the old sensor does not).** With a 2.4 N error (2 sigma of the pre-2017 FT 300) and k_t known, the same case leaves more than 0.3 mm. Refuted if 0.3 mm or less.
  - **H134 (the stiffness must be known too).** With an exact sensor and k_t misjudged by 25% (either way), a 5 N drag leaves more than 0.3 mm along the wall at the worst G1 pose. Refuted if 0.3 mm or less in both directions of the error.
  - **H135 (the stack recovers).** With the current FT 300's noise and k_t known, a drag uniform over 0-20 N costs the G1 at most 2 points of stack at the worst pose (milestone 35, unmodelled: 91.6). Refuted if more.

`src/buildbot_sheathing/hose.py` (new: catenary hanging length and touchdown, the floor's drag limit, the force at the fitting), `experiments/m35_hose/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_hose.py` (5 tests), `reproduce.py` (m35 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Checks.** A zero hose force reproduces the no-hose torques exactly (0.0e+00 N m); the catenary's closed-form length matches a numerical arc length of the curve to 1e-6 (test). Milestone 34 re-ran character for character in this session's environment (mujoco 4.0.1) before this run.
- **The force (A).** From the listed weights, a hanging hose pulls 0.57–2.38 N (hybrid) or 1.04–4.30 N (rubber) down at the grip over the poses' grip heights (0.39–1.19 m on the G1), i.e. 0.06–0.44 kg. The floor holds 0.9–11.9 N (hybrid) and 1.6–21.5 N (rubber) of horizontal tension over mu_f 0.3–0.8 and 2–10 m dragged; a typical case (mu_f 0.5, 5 m) is 3.7 and 6.7 N.
- **Transmission windup (B; H130 held).** At k_t 1e4 N m/rad a 5 N pull in the worst direction moves the G1's free nose 1.25 mm in the wall plane (rubber; hybrid 1.07), 2.09 mm at 10 N, 2.49 mm at 20 N; hanging alone 0.33 mm. H1 1.20 mm at 5 N, 3.01 mm at 20 N. The encoders do not see it.
- **The worst direction is towards the wall (H131 refuted).** At 5 N the worst G1 pose winds up 1.251 mm for a pull towards the wall (phi 0), 1.131 mm at 315°, and at most 0.921 mm along the wall (270°). A pull along the wall normal acts like a change of press, which milestone 34 found the costliest load.
- **The matched compensator (C).** With the hose unmodelled and one calibration at milestone 30's pose (hose in the same state there): hanging rubber hose 0.474 mm along the wall at the worst G1 pose, from 3 joint-pose cases missed (H128 refuted; m33's +0.4 kg magazine left 0.017 mm because its mispredicted joints had short levers, and the hose's weight also changes with grip height); 5 N 1.001 mm (rubber) and 1.082 mm (hybrid) (H129 held), with 1/9/13 wrong/spurious/missed; 10 N 1.9 mm. H1: 0.18 mm hanging, 1.17 mm at 5 N, 1.4–1.5 mm at 10–20 N.
- **Stacks (D).** 12 bits, 5 arcmin, k_t 1e4, the rubber hose's tension uniform over [0, H_max] in C's worst direction, the backlash residual and mean windup at H_max/2 as a bias and the windup's spread as a scatter: the worst pose loses 2.17 points at H_max 5 N (H1 4.39), 20.7 at 10 N (H1 34.3) and 91.6 at 20 N (H1 67.8; G1 on the 13 poses that balance).
- **Balance.** 'hold-min' has no solution in 19–22 of the G1's 120 pose-direction cases at 20 N and in up to 33 of the H1's: a hard drag is a balance load too.
- **Servo droop (E).** Under Unitree's PD gains the hanging hose droops the G1's free nose 29–53 mm, a 5 N pull 175–205 mm, 20 N 369–406 mm (H1 18–32, 134–152, 354–382 mm). The encoders see this; re-targeting on them removes it, as in milestone 34.
- **What it means.** Milestone 33's rule (compensate backlash with the robot's own controller model) survives a magazine but not a hose: the hose is an unknown, pose- and history-dependent force, and above about 2 N of drag it leaves more than the 0.3 mm a compensator was meant to reach. A spec has three options: take the hose off the tool (a boom or balancer that brings it in from above, so H stays near 0 and its weight is carried elsewhere); measure the tool's wrench (a wrist force-torque sensor would turn the hose into a known load; not modelled here); or keep the drag under about 2–5 N (short hose on the floor, a slick floor, a hose reel that pays out). A hose dragged across 10 m of floor can cost 20–90 points.
- **Not measured.** Hose weights are listings (fittings included); floor friction and dragged length are swept; the hose's bending stiffness and its own torsion are neglected; the fitting is taken at the grip (on a framing nailer it sits at the end of the handle); H is the same at the calibration pose and the nail; backlash flips within the H range are taken at H_max/2 only (the stack's bias); statics only; the H1's 0.4 m, −0.15 m pose has no 'hold-min' solution and is skipped.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec (backlash, compensation, windup, hose); a wrist force-torque sensor as the remedy; the nailer's recoil.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 35 planned: the air hose as a force on the tool (its hanging weight and its drag along the floor)

- **Why.** Milestones 33–34 treated the hose only as mass (a 1.0 kg unsourced allowance). A hose is not a mass: it hangs from the tool's air fitting to the floor and, once the robot has walked along the wall, the length lying on the floor drags behind it. At the fitting it pulls down with the weight of the hanging length and sideways with whatever horizontal tension the floor friction holds, in the direction the hose runs. Neither the compensator (milestone 33) nor a fixed feedforward knows that tension, and it changes from nail to nail as the robot moves.
- **Sources.** 3/8 in × 50 ft hoses, as listed: hybrid polymer (Flexzilla HFZ3850YW2, 5.1 lb, toolsource.com; Flexair, 5.08 lb, lawsonproducts.com) and rubber (Workforce, 9.21 lb, kbctools.ca; Tekton, 7.30 lb), so w = 0.152 kg/m (hybrid) and 0.274 kg/m (rubber), fittings included. The hose-on-floor friction coefficient and the dragged length are not sourced: mu_f swept 0.3–0.8, length 0–10 m.
- **Model.** The hanging part is a catenary from the fitting (at the grip, height h above the floor) to a touchdown point where it lies flat: with horizontal tension H and weight per length w g, the hanging length is s = sqrt(h² + 2hH/(wg)) and the fitting carries (H cos phi, H sin phi, −wg s). H runs from 0 (hose lying loose) to mu_f w g L_drag (the floor length about to slide). The force acts at the grip; the nose's press is unchanged. On milestone 32's leaned poses, robot on 'hold-min' (milestone 33's matched case): (A) the force per pose; (B) its transmission windup at k_t 1e4 (free nose, before contact; invisible to the encoders); (C) the matched backlash compensator's residual and mispredictions with the hose unmodelled, H from 0 to 20 N, phi swept; (D) the stack with H uniform over [0, H_max] as a scatter; (E) servo droop under Unitree's gains (visible to the encoders), for reference.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only the hose listings above and milestones 32–34's published numbers have been read; no hose force, windup, residual or stack has been computed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H128 (a hanging hose is a magazine).** With H = 0 the rubber hose at the worst G1 pose leaves the matched compensator a residual of at most 0.1 mm along the wall after one calibration. Refuted if more.
  - **H129 (a dragged hose is not).** A 5 N horizontal pull in the worst direction leaves the matched compensator more than 0.3 mm along the wall at the worst G1 pose. Refuted if 0.3 mm or less.
  - **H130 (the drag winds up the joints).** At k_t 1e4 N m/rad, a 5 N horizontal pull in the worst direction moves the G1's free nose by more than 0.5 mm in the wall plane at the worst pose. Refuted if 0.5 mm or less.
  - **H131 (along the wall is worst).** Of the horizontal directions, a pull along the wall (±y) gives the largest windup at the nose at the worst G1 pose. Refuted if another direction (away from or towards the wall, or diagonal) is larger by more than 10%.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 34 result: a nose free to slide needs joints stiffer than about 1.8e4 N m/rad; friction holds it at 10 of 15 G1 poses; an unmodelled magazine droops the servo by 32–51 mm; H124 refuted, H125–H127 held

`src/buildbot_sheathing/windup.py` (new: free windup, the nose stuck in the wall plane, the friction-limited slide, the stick test, the press scatter added to a `ToolError`), `experiments/m34_windup/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_windup.py` (5 tests), `reproduce.py` (m34 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph, the tool-point limitation, the conclusion), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **A change after the first run (disclosed).** The first run pinned all three of the nose's translations. The pinning reaction then cancelled the press itself (friction ratios up to 5e9): along the wall normal the press is force-controlled, so x cannot be pinned. The model now pins only y and z (the controller closes any x windup with a commanded motion along x) and adds a friction-limited case: the stuck reaction grows with the press from zero, so the ratio |lam|/F is fixed per pose, and above mu the nose slides 1 − mu/ratio of its free windup (reaction taken along the stuck direction). H124 is stated about the in-plane reaction, so it is tested as registered; the first run's numbers are not used.
- **Checks.** The stuck nose's in-plane windup is 1e-15 mm (zero); the sticking reaction differs by 1.8e-15 N between k_t 3e3 and 1e5 (a common k_t cancels, as derived).
- **Windup over the 25–50 N window, free nose.** Worst G1 pose (1.2 m, +0.15 m): 10.6 mm at k_t 3e3, 3.19 mm at 1e4, 1.06 mm at 3e4, 0.32 mm at 1e5 (H1 3.47 mm at 1e4). Stuck, the nail's tip at 44.4 mm moves 0.018 mm at 1e4 (H1 0.037 mm): the tool only rotates about the nose.
- **Does the nose stick (H124 refuted).** The G1 needs a friction coefficient of 0.073–0.789 (median 0.383); at mu 0.4 it sticks at 10 of 15 poses (0.2: 4; 0.8: all 15). The worst is 1.2 m, −0.15 m. H1 0.026–1.166, 10 of 20 at mu 0.4.
- **Stacks (12 bits, 5 arcmin, matched backlash compensation).** At k_t 1e4 the press window costs the G1 5.47 points with a free nose (H125 held), 0.00 stuck (H126 held), 1.05 friction-limited at mu 0.4 (H1 4.64, 0.01, 1.82). Within 2 points at the worst pose takes k_t ≥ 1.78e4 N m/rad free and ≥ 1.0e4 at mu 0.4 (grid of quarter decades), for both robots. Below about 6e3 the stack collapses (G1 free: 23.5 points lost at 5.6e3, 80.5 at 3.2e3).
- **The mean windup is a pose bias.** At 37.5 N, one calibration at milestone 30's pose leaves 1.76 mm along the wall and 12.4 mm vertically at k_t 1e4 (G1; z worst at 0.4 m, where the knees and hips carry the most). It is linear in the torques, so a model computes it; with k_t misjudged by 25% a quarter is left (3.1 mm vertically at 1e4).
- **Servo droop (H127 held).** A full magazine left out of the feedforward moves the G1's free nose by 32.1–51.3 mm under Unitree's gains (worst 0.4 m, −0.15 m, nearly all vertical), mostly through the waist pitch and the ankle pitches (60 and 40 N m/rad); H1 17.3–22.4 mm. The servo's error is what the joint encoders read, so a robot that re-targets on its encoders, or counts the nails it has fired, removes it; one that trusts PD with a fixed feedforward does not.
- **What it means.** Three rules for a tool-point spec: fire on a known force or let friction hold the nose (a stuck nose turns the press's windup into a 0.02 mm tilt); ask for joints stiffer than about 1e4 N m/rad (2e4 if the nose can slide), behind the encoder; and close the loop on the encoders, since the servo is about a hundred times softer (40–150 N m/rad). A gripping nose tip (teeth, rubber) is the cheapest of the three.
- **Not measured.** No torsional stiffness is published for these robots, so k_t is swept and the same on every joint; links and the tool mount are rigid; the nose-to-OSB friction is swept; the slide is approximated along the stuck reaction's direction; torques are taken as linear from zero press at contact; the press scatter enters the stack as a Gaussian of the uniform's variance with the y–z correlation dropped; statics only (no recoil). The H1's 0.4 m, −0.15 m pose has no 'hold-min' solution and is skipped.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec (now with backlash, compensation and windup); the hose as a force; the nailer's recoil.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 34 planned: deflection under the press (the joints' elastic windup, the servo's droop, and whether the nose sticks)

- **Why.** "Deflection under the press" has been the open item of milestones 29–33. Every joint so far was rigid apart from its backlash gap. A joint also twists elastically: through its *servo* (Unitree's published PD gains, `humanoid.GAINS`: G1 hips 100, knees 150, ankles 40, waist and arms 60 N m/rad) against any torque the controller does not feed forward, and through its *transmission* (gearbox and structure, stiffness k_t) against all of it. The press is the load that changes between the nose touching the sheet and the shot (anywhere in 25–50 N, milestone 30), so its windup is not a fixed bias a calibration or milestone 33's model can remove unless the shot's force is known.
- **Sources looked for.** No published torsional stiffness was found for the G1's or H1's joints (a search on 2026-11-16 found catalogue figures for strain-wave gears and one measured value for a 3D-printed planetary prototype, neither for these robots, and the catalogue host does not answer from this session). So k_t is swept, 3e3 to 1e5 N m/rad on every joint, and the result is stated as the stiffness a spec must ask for. The nose-to-OSB friction coefficient is not sourced either; mu swept 0.2–0.8, 0.4 the registered case.
- **Plan.** `src/buildbot_sheathing/windup.py`: free windup -dtau/k; the nose *stuck* by friction (its three translations pinned: the reaction lam = (A K^-1 A^T)^-1 A K^-1 dtau, the windup survives only as a rotation of the tool about the nose, seen at the 44.4 mm depth point); the stick test |lam_yz| <= mu (F - lam_x). On milestone 32's leaned poses (G1 15, H1 20–21), with the robot balancing on 'hold-min' (milestone 33's matched case): (A) the windup over the 25–50 N press per pose, free and stuck, per k_t; (B) whether the nose sticks (per newton of press, from contact at zero press; with the same k on every joint lam does not depend on k); (C) the stack (single 2x, 12 bits, 5 arcmin backlash on loose joints, held joints compensated by the matched model) with the press uniform over the window, as a Gaussian of the same variance, against milestone 33's matched stack; the k_t that keeps the worst pose within 2 points; (D) the mean windup at 37.5 N as a pose bias: after one calibration, and after model compensation with k_t misjudged by 25%; (E) servo droop: a full 64-nail magazine of 8d nails (0.295 kg, milestone 33) left out of the feedforward, under Unitree's gains, at the nose (free, before contact).
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only the code above and milestones 30–33's published numbers have been read; no windup, reaction, stick ratio, droop or stack has been computed at any pose.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H124 (the nose sticks).** At mu 0.4, the reaction that keeps the G1's nose from sliding as the press grows stays inside the friction cone at every leaned G1 pose. Refuted if it leaves the cone at any pose.
  - **H125 (a sliding nose pays for the press).** With the nose free and k_t = 1e4 N m/rad on every joint, the press window costs the 12-bit G1 more than 2 points of stack at the worst leaned pose. Refuted if 2 points or less.
  - **H126 (sticking saves it).** With the nose stuck and the same k_t, the press window costs at most 1 point at the worst leaned pose. Refuted if more than 1.
  - **H127 (the servo droops).** Under Unitree's PD gains with the listed 3.4 kg tool fed forward, an unmodelled full magazine (0.295 kg) moves the G1's free nose by more than 1 mm in the wall plane at the worst leaned pose. Refuted if 1 mm or less.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 33 result: model-based backlash compensation removes the residual only if it models the balance controller; then a full magazine costs 0.02 mm; H121 held, H122 and H123 refuted

`src/buildbot_sheathing/compensate.py` (new: the predicted bias with an optional guard band, misprediction classes, the residual after compensation and one calibration), `experiments/m33_compensate/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_compensate.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m33 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph, conclusion), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **Checks.** The matched compensator leaves 0.00e+00 mm and the worst-pose stack equals milestone 32's per-pose calibration (G1 3.74 points on 'hold-min', 4.69 on 'torque'); with nothing predicted ('none') the residual reproduces milestone 32's one calibration, −1.401 mm at 1.2 m, −0.15 m.
- **The unmodelled load, computed.** A 64-nail magazine (Metabo HPT listing) of 8d common nails (2-1/2 × 0.131 in) is 0.295 kg of steel, of 16d (3-1/2 × 0.131 in) 0.410 kg; +1.0 kg is an unsourced allowance for the collation and the hose.
- **The controller must be in the model (H121 held).** With the G1 balancing on 'hold-min', a compensator that assumes the least-norm split leaves 1.156 mm at 0.4 m, +0.15 m (17 joint-pose cases on the wrong side, 2 spurious, 47 missed over 15 poses); assuming 'force' 1.450 mm, 'torque' 1.237 mm, against 1.401 mm with no compensation at all. With the G1 on 'torque', wrong models leave 1.237–1.924 mm (no compensation 2.632). A wrong ground-reaction model buys at most 0.25 mm on the G1 when the robot holds its light joints. On the H1 the same mismatch leaves 0.475–1.128 mm (12–38 joint-pose cases on the wrong side).
- **A full magazine (H122 refuted, on the count).** With the controller modelled ('hold-min'), +0.4 kg mispredicts 2 joint-pose cases (1 spurious, 1 missed) and leaves 0.017 mm, so the registered "no misprediction" fails while the 0.3 mm half holds; +1.0 kg leaves 0.057 mm (4 spurious, 6 missed; 3.80 points lost at the worst pose against 3.74 matched). The 'torque' controller is far more sensitive: +0.2 kg already leaves 0.412 mm (7.58 points at that pose) and +1.0 kg 0.836 mm (19.40 points). It leaves light joints wherever the optimum puts them, often near zero, while 'hold-min' keeps them at tau_f or more. H1 matched ('hold-min'): 0.179 mm at +0.2 and +0.4 kg, 0.435 mm at +1.0 kg.
- **A guard band does not help (H123 refuted).** Compensating only predicted |tau| >= 1 N m leaves 1.374 mm for the least-norm compensator (wrong sides fall from 17 to 11, but missed joints rise from 47 to 79, each leaving half a gap). On the matched compensator a band of 0.5–2 N m turns 0 into 0.961–1.351 mm. A joint left uncompensated costs as much as a guessed one.
- **What it means.** The remedy milestones 31–32 named works, and works robustly, on one condition: the compensator predicts torques with the robot's own balance controller (the same QP, the same held set). Then an unmodelled magazine is invisible (0.017 mm) and even +1 kg costs 0.057 mm, provided the controller keeps the light leg joints one way ('hold-min'). The design rule is a sign-holding controller paired with compensation computed from that controller; a generic model of the robot's statics is not enough.
- **Not measured.** Statics only; the "true" robot is still the model (its gains, joint friction and gap asymmetry are not measured); the poses do not re-lean for extra mass; the H1's 0.4 m, −0.15 m pose has no 'hold-min' solution and is skipped (20 of 21 poses). Mass enters at the tool only.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec; deflection under the press; the hose as a force rather than a mass.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 33 planned: model-based backlash compensation, and what model error it survives (the controller, the magazine)

- **Why.** Milestones 31–32 concluded that one tool-point calibration cannot remove the held joints' backlash along a wall (1.1–2.6 mm left on the leaned G1 poses) and named the remedy: predict each held joint's side from the static torques at every pose and subtract the predicted bias in joint space. Neither milestone tested that remedy. A compensator predicts with *its* model, and two parts of that model are uncertain on a real robot: which ground-reaction split the balance controller picks (milestone 32: leg torques differ by tens of N m between splits, waist and arm torques not at all), and the tool's mass (the 3.4 kg listing is the empty tool; the magazine holds 64 nails, Metabo HPT product page, and the air hose hangs off it).
- **Plan.** `src/buildbot_sheathing/compensate.py`: the predicted bias delta_hat(p) from a model's torques (held set and sides at tau_f 0.2 over the 25–50 N press, optionally only joints whose predicted |tau| >= a guard band tau_g at both press ends), the true bias delta(p) from the robot's torques, and the residual after compensation plus one tool-frame calibration at milestone 30's pose: mu(p) = A(p)[delta(p) − delta_hat(p)] − A(ref)[delta(ref) − delta_hat(ref)]. Counted per pose: joints mispredicted (wrong side, or predicted held but loose, or held but not compensated). On milestone 32's 15 leaned G1 poses (21 H1), 12 bits, 5 arcmin: (A) matched model (check: residual 0, stack equal to per-pose calibration); (B) controller mismatch: robot runs 'hold-min' or 'torque', compensator assumes 'free', 'force' or 'torque'; (C) mass mismatch: the robot carries 0, +0.2, +0.4 and +1.0 kg the compensator does not know (the magazine's nails computed from steel density and nail size, the 1.0 kg a hose allowance, not sourced); (D) guard band tau_g 0.2–2 N m on the mismatched cases; the stack per pose for each.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only milestone 32's published numbers and the code have been read; no compensated residual, misprediction count or stack has been computed.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H121 (the controller must be in the model).** A compensator that assumes the least-norm 'free' split while the G1 runs 'hold-min' leaves more than 1 mm along the wall at the worst grid pose after compensation and one calibration. Refuted if 1 mm or less.
  - **H122 (a full magazine flips nothing).** With the controller modelled correctly ('hold-min'), an unmodelled +0.4 kg at the tool mispredicts no G1 joint at any grid pose and leaves at most 0.3 mm. Refuted if any joint is mispredicted or the worst residual exceeds 0.3 mm.
  - **H123 (a guard band rescues a wrong controller model).** For H121's mismatched compensator, compensating only joints with predicted |tau| >= 1 N m brings the worst residual to 1 mm or less. Refuted if it stays above 1 mm.

`src/buildbot_sheathing/grf_qp.py` (new: sole contact points from the Menagerie collision geoms, friction-pyramid LP and QPs, centre of pressure, press window, leaned pose), `experiments/m32_grf_qp/` (run, results, figure), `tests/test_grf_qp.py` (4 tests), `reproduce.py` (m32 listed), `paper/draft.md` (section 5.11 paragraph, the tool-point limitation, the conclusion), `paper/check_numbers.py`, README, PROPOSAL and project card.

- **The press needs a lean (not in the plan; found by the first solve).** With each sole pushing only through its contact points (G1: four 5 mm spheres; H1: both ends of three capsules) and mu 0.5, milestones 30–31's upright poses balance at most 79.6 N at a 0.4 m hand height, 39.0 N at 0.8 m, 31.1 N at 1.0 m and 25.9 N at 1.2 m (G1; H1 100.9 to 25.2 N over 0.4–1.6 m). The free 6-D foot wrench had hidden this: above about 0.8 m the 50 N press tips the robot back onto its heels. Leaning the centre of mass 0.040–0.124 m towards the wall (secant on `com_dx` until the centre of pressure sits mid-sole at 37.5 N) brings the whole 25–50 N range inside the window at all 36 poses, so every number below is on the leaned poses. The plan did not include this; it changes the poses, so milestone 31's numbers are not directly comparable.
- **Structural check.** Waist and arm torques are identical under all five ground-reaction models (largest difference 0.00e+00 N m): their subtrees hold no foot.
- **The flips (H118 refuted).** On the leaned poses neither hip yaw nor the waist roll flips under any model, including milestone 31's least-norm one; the only G1 joint held everywhere that flips is the right wrist pitch (−6.04 to +3.00 N m), an arm joint no controller can touch. Milestone 31's flips came from the upright poses, not from the ground-reaction split.
- **Holding every leg joint (H119 refuted).** Keeping all 12 G1 leg joints on one side by 0.2 N m is infeasible at all 15 poses (worst LP margin −45.5 N m: hip pitches and knees carry tens of N m whose sign the lean sets). **H120** asked about the residual under that controller and is not testable.
- **Holding the light leg joints (added after the first run, not registered).** Holding only the hip rolls, hip yaws and ankle rolls (6 G1 joints) is feasible at every pose (worst margin 3.31 N m). Calibrated per pose, the worst 5-arcmin loss falls from 8.63 points (least-norm) and 4.69 (torque-minimising) to 3.74. With one calibration at the 1.0 m pose the worst offset along the wall is 1.401 mm (least-norm 1.950, force 2.056, torque 2.632) and the worst pose still loses 59.59 points.
- **The calibration pose (not registered).** The 1.0 m pose is where most G1 joints are loose under the least-norm split (knees, waist roll and pitch). Searching every grid pose as the calibration pose: least-norm 1.949 mm at best (0.8 m), torque 1.910, light-joint hold 1.094 mm (calibrated at 1.2 m), still 36.67 points lost at the worst pose. What remains comes from joints held at some poses and loose at others, and from the wrist pitch.
- **Sweeps.** mu 0.3–0.8 and tau_f 0.05–0.5: the full hold is infeasible everywhere; the light-joint hold is feasible at 10/15 poses at mu 0.3 and 14–15/15 otherwise, with worst one-calibration offsets 1.29–2.18 mm. At tau_f 0.05 the waist yaw and roll flip again under the force model.
- **H1.** The torque-minimising controller does best: one calibration loses at most 6.65 points (0.669 mm); least-norm 65.03 points.
- **What it means.** A balance controller cannot remove the need for pose-by-pose compensation, but it is what makes the press possible at all above chest height, and holding the light leg joints halves the per-pose backlash loss. The engineering rule is unchanged from milestone 31 (predict each held joint's side from the static torques at every pose), and the poses it applies to must be the leaned ones.
- **Not measured.** Statics only: no balance dynamics or the nailer's recoil. Contact points are the model's collision geoms (a real sole's patch differs); mu swept 0.3–0.8. The QP holds a joint at exactly tau_f, the least a real controller would allow. The lean is set at 37.5 N; a controller that re-leans per press would change the held sets.
- **Next.** The paper's headline for a tool-point spec; deflection under the press; whether re-leaning with the press (a centre-of-pressure servo) keeps the ankle pitch held.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 32 planned: the sign flips under a balance controller's ground reaction (friction cones, the sole's corners)

- **Why.** Milestone 31 found three G1 joints held at every pose that flip sign along the wall (both hip yaws and the waist roll), so one tool-point calibration leaves up to 3.4 mm, and the two ankle rolls are loose at every pose. All of it used the least-norm split of a free 6-D wrench at one point per foot, which no robot applies: a real sole pushes only through its contact patch (unilateral normal force, friction cone, centre of pressure inside the sole), and a balance controller picks one force distribution out of the many that balance the body. Milestone 31's "Not measured" named this.
- **A structural point, stated before running.** Joint torque in a static balance is set by the loads on the joint's subtree. With the pelvis as the floating base, the waist and arm joints' subtrees hold the torso, arms and nailer but no foot, so *no* choice of ground reaction changes their torque: the waist-roll flip survives every controller. The leg joints (hip yaw among them, and the ankle rolls) carry the ground reaction and can be shaped. The run checks this numerically (waist and arm torques equal across controllers to 1e-9 N m) rather than assuming it.
- **Plan.** `src/buildbot_sheathing/grf_qp.py`: (A) each foot's contact points from the Menagerie model's own collision geoms (G1: four 5 mm spheres per sole; H1: three capsules per sole, both ends), each with a 3-D force, normal force at least 0, a friction pyramid at mu 0.5 (swept 0.3–0.8); (B) three controllers, each a QP over those forces that balances the floating base exactly: *force-regularised* (min sum of squared contact forces, the usual whole-body-control regulariser), *torque-minimising* (min squared leg-joint torque, plus a 1e-6 force regulariser), and *sign-holding* (torque-minimising, plus every leg joint that moves the nose kept on one side, |tau| >= tau_f, with the side per joint chosen from the torque-minimising solution at milestone 30's pose, both sides tried for joints near zero there); (C) for each controller, milestone 31's grid, held sets, sign flips, per-pose stack and one-calibration residual (split into its Jacobian and flip parts), at 12 bits, 5 arcmin, a 25–50 N press, tau_f 0.2 (swept 0.05–0.5); (D) where the sign-holding QP is infeasible, which constraint binds.
- **Disclosed before registering.** Only the Menagerie collision geometry has been looked at (the G1 sole points are at x −0.05 and +0.12 m, y ±0.025–0.03 m). No QP has been solved and no torque, held set or stack number exists under any of these controllers.
- **Hypotheses, registered before the run.**
  - **H118 (cones alone keep the flips).** Under the force-regularised controller with friction cones (mu 0.5), at least one of the G1's hip yaws still flips sign across the grid while held at every pose. Refuted if neither hip yaw does.
  - **H119 (a controller can hold the legs).** The sign-holding controller is feasible at every G1 grid pose at both press ends (mu 0.5, tau_f 0.2). Refuted if any pose is infeasible.
  - **H120 (the waist keeps the millimetres).** Under the sign-holding controller (where feasible), one tool-point calibration at milestone 30's pose still leaves more than 1 mm along the wall at the worst G1 pose. Refuted if 1 mm or less.
